# Gotchas

**Read this when something surprises you, before you start debugging it.** Every
entry cost somebody real time once. Finding yours here turns an afternoon into a
minute, and that is the whole return on the
[correction ratchet](correction-ratchet.md).

Entries are indexed by the **symptom you actually arrive with**, because you
arrive with an error message and not with a category. Search this page for the
string you are seeing.

## Tooling and environment

| Symptom | Cause | Do this |
|---|---|---|
| In a git worktree without its own `.venv`, `pytest` run with the main checkout's venv fails a test of code your branch changed with an error from the OLD code (`AttributeError: 'CapturedPage' object has no attribute 'pages'`), while the tests around it pass | `pyproject.toml`'s `pythonpath` puts this checkout's `packages/agent/src` and `apps/agent-host/src` first, and nothing else: `public_sources`, `knowledge_layer` and the other workspace packages are editable installs, so they resolve to the checkout the venv was built in | Build the worktree its own venv (`UV_LINK_MODE=copy uv sync --group dev` in the worktree, about a minute) and run `.venv/bin/pytest` from there; `task` does that by itself once the venv exists |
| A commit in a worktree lists `A  .venv` (a one-line file) after `git add -A`, and the PR's diff shows `.venv | 1 +` | A worktree borrows the main checkout's venv as a symlink, for the hooks; `.gitignore` said `.venv/`, and a pattern with a trailing slash matches only a directory, never a symlink | Fixed in `.gitignore`: the pattern is `.venv` (and `venv`), with no slash. On an older branch, `git rm --cached .venv` and commit; never rewrite a pushed branch for it |
| CI fails broadly on a pull request that adds a route (`Failed to resolve import "@/app/api/…/coverage/route"` in several Workspace shards, the frontend image build, the bff-jobs bundle, `agent-reachability.spec.ts`), while every check passes locally | An unanchored directory pattern in a `.gitignore` (`coverage/` in `apps/workspace/.gitignore`, meant for vitest's output) also ignores an app route folder of that name, so the route file was never committed; git add says nothing, and the local tree still has it (PR 1574) | Anchor output patterns to their folder (`/coverage/`); `tests/test_gitignore_anchored.py` now fails any unanchored directory pattern whose name could be a source folder. Before pushing a new route, `git check-ignore -v <path>` or `git status --short` shows a new file that will not be committed |
| A deploy fails in `Pulumi up`: the Job `grid-app-authz-catalog` is in `CrashLoopBackOff`, exit code 1, and its log ends with `1 drift finding(s)` and `UNKNOWN <slug> — in WorkOS, absent from the catalog` | A permission was removed from `apps/workspace/bff/authz/catalog.ts` and the WorkOS environment still has it. The catalog script exits 1 on any drift, in apply mode too, and an unknown slug in one of our namespaces is drift (#1594 removed `platform:knowledge:approve_as_second_reviewer`, 2026-11-10; every later merge to develop stayed undeployed) | Add the slug to `RETIRED_PERMISSIONS` in `apps/workspace/bff/authz/retired-permissions.ts`: the next deploy takes it off every role and deletes it. Do not delete it in the dashboard. Remove and retire in one change ([Retiring a permission](../deployment/workos-provisioning.md#retiring-a-permission)). CI cannot see it: the environment is remote state |
| The dev deploy fails with `Deployment public-source-fetcher updating failed … ImagePullBackOff … failed to fetch anonymous token … 401 Unauthorized` for the backend image on `ghcr.io` | A workload outside the shared `AppWiring` (installed by its own `install…` call in `deploy/pulumi/index.ts`) was given no `imagePullSecrets`, so it pulls the private backend image anonymously (the public-source fetcher, made mandatory in #1592) | Pass the registry pull Secret to every pod that runs an app image, as `AppWiring.imagePullSecrets` does; `installPublicSourceFetcher` now requires it, and `deploy/pulumi/index-pull-secrets.spec.ts` fails any pod running a private image without one |
| The dev deploy fails in `Pulumi up (dev)` with `NetworkPolicy … is invalid: spec.egress[…].ipBlock.except[…]: Invalid value: "::ffff:0:0/96": must be a strict subset of cidr`, and every later merge to develop stays undeployed | The Kubernetes API server parses an IPv4-mapped IPv6 block as IPv4, so under `cidr: "::/0"` it is no subset. Unit specs and `pulumi preview` against the mocks accept it; only the real API server refuses it (the public-source fetcher's policy, created for the first time when #1592 made the fetcher mandatory, 2026-11-10) | Keep IPv4-mapped ranges out of IPv6 `ipBlock.except`: a mapped address leaves as IPv4, where the IPv4 block's `except` holds it. `public-source-fetcher.spec.ts` now fails on a `::ffff:` entry. After a merge, check the deploy run, not only CI: a red deploy blocks every merge after it |
| Switching on a Pulumi workload that was off by default (`publicSourceFetcherEnabled`, any `…Enabled: false`) fails `index-policy.spec.ts` (`deployment-rollout-gated: minReadySeconds`, `workload-graceful-termination`) and `index-node-groups.spec.ts` (`expected [ 'Deployment/<name>' ] to deeply equal []`), though its own spec passed when it was written | Those two specs check only what the dev and prod plans render. A Deployment no stack deploys is never measured against the policy pack's mandatory rules or the node-group placement, so its gaps stay hidden until the day it is switched on (the public-source fetcher, 2026-11-10) | Give an off-by-default workload `onNodeGroup`, a surge rollout and `gracefulShutdown` when you write it, as `gotenberg.ts` does, not when it is turned on. To see the gaps early, run those two specs with the switch set in their stack config |
| `task workspace:blast-radius` (Workspace job) fails with `<module>: a new hub, reached by N specs (limit M)` after you added one `import` to a module whose importers you did not change, and `--top` on develop shows the same N | `scripts/blast-radius.mjs` holds a leaf (a module that imports nothing of ours) by neither rule. Your import made a wide leaf a non-leaf, so its existing reach became a finding: `wire/vocab.ts` read 621 specs on develop too (PR 1596) | Keep the leaf a leaf: import the new thing where it is used (`wire/source.ts` imports the generated source lists itself), not through the leaf. Compare with a develop worktree's `--top 400` before raising the baseline |
| CI's Website job fails in a second with `Node.js v22.23.3 is not supported by Astro! Please upgrade Node.js to a supported version: ">=22.23.4"`, on a pull request that touched nothing of the site, while the same job passed an hour earlier | `setup-node` with `node-version: "22"` takes whatever 22.x the runner image has cached, and an older image still in the pool had 22.23.3 (PR 1477, run 41725318204; that runner also had git 2.55 where the others had 2.56). Which Node a job got depended on which runner it landed on | Every CI job reads `.node-version` (`node-version-file`), an exact version; `setup-node` downloads it when the runner lacks it. A dependency that raises its Node floor is a change to that one file. `tests/test_ci_workflows.py` fails a job that asks for a floating `22` |
| A UI spec passes alone and under `--pool=forks`, and fails in the suite on an `instanceof` (`The first argument must be of type string or an instance of Buffer`, an `AbortError` not recognised as a `DOMException`, `Object.defineProperty called on non-object` from `x instanceof URL`) | The suite runs in vitest's vm pool: each spec file has its own realm, and an object Node itself made (a socket's `Buffer`, a `URL`, a built-in's `DOMException`) belongs to the outer one | Test by shape instead of `instanceof` (`isFileUrl` in `config/vitest/disk-inputs.ts`). If the check is production code that is right as it is, add the spec to `PROCESS_PER_FILE` in `apps/workspace/vitest.config.ts` with the reason; it then runs in a process of its own |
| A UI spec's spy on a DOM method (`Element.prototype.scrollTo = vi.fn()`) is never called on one CI shard, and the spec passes alone and in the same shard locally | The vm pool gives each spec file its own realm but loads happy-dom once per worker, so `HTMLElement.prototype` is shared by every file the worker runs. A spec that "restored" an inherited method by assigning what it read left an own property on `HTMLElement.prototype`, which shadows the next file's spy on `Element.prototype` | Restore a patched DOM method by its own-property descriptor, deleting it when there was none. `config/vitest/polyfills.ts` resets the methods specs stub (`STUBBED_METHODS`: the scroll methods and `getBoundingClientRect`) on `Element.prototype` and `HTMLElement.prototype` per file; a method outside that list needs adding there. Reproduce with `--maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism <leaking spec> <failing spec>` |
| `bff/inbound-mail/suggest-job.spec.ts` passes alone and fails in the full run with `reason: 'switched_off'` where it expects `nothing_to_place` (8 cases), or `bff/filing/trigger.spec.ts` fails `queues neither review for a batch opened without filing reviews` (no batch queued): `FILING_SUGGESTIONS=off` is in force in tests that do not set it (other tests in both files set it to `off` and restore their stubs) | **Not established.** Seen twice on 2026-11-10, then not again in two full runs and in many paired runs. Ruled out: `config/vitest/polyfills.ts` does reset `process.env` per file (the baseline lives on the shared `process`; checked with two throwaway specs and one worker); one vm worker runs its files one after another, never interleaved; the two specs in either order in one worker pass. vitest keeps `vi.stubEnv`'s originals in one map per worker, so a file that ends with a stub still open is a candidate. `unstubEnvs: true` / `unstubGlobals: true` in `vitest.config.ts` do not cure it and break the suites that stub in `beforeAll` (the door suites stub `fetch`, `BACKEND_URL` and `INTERNAL_API_TOKEN` once) | Rerun the file alone and the full run once; if it fails again, record which files ran before it on the same worker: `--reporter=verbose` prints results, not worker ids, so log `process.pid` and the file path at the start of each file (a `setupFiles` line), and add the order here. Do not add `unstubEnvs`/`unstubGlobals` without moving every `beforeAll` stub into `beforeEach` first |
| A UI spec that renders an answer card fails with `Unable to find an element with the text: <the card's title>` and a `data-slot="card-placeholder"` skeleton in the dump, on one CI shard, on a loaded runner, or every time when run alone, and passes in other runs | `A2uiCard` loads its renderer (`react-catalog`) through a dynamic `import()`. Until it settles a card draws its placeholder, so a spec that checks the card in the commit it renders passes only when an earlier spec in the same worker already loaded the renderer (`CardSlotArrival.spec.tsx`, `answer-card-placement.spec.tsx`, red on develop's shard 7/8 from 55441e4d) | `beforeAll(rendererSettled)`, imported from `@/features/a2ui/A2uiCard`, at file level, as `AnswerCards.render-coverage.spec.tsx` does. Reproduce by running the spec alone (the cold case), or while `task verify:fast` loads the machine |
| After adding a vitest `projects` entry with `extends: true`, the suite takes twice as long and reports twice the spec files | A project that extends the root config ADDS its `include` and `exclude` to the root's lists instead of replacing them, so a project meant for seven files ran all of them | Keep `include` and `exclude` out of the root `test` block and state them in every project (`SPEC_FILES` in `apps/workspace/vitest.config.ts`). Check the file count in the summary line against `find` |
| A test or hook that scans the tree with `git grep -E` takes many seconds, and adding patterns makes it worse, not better (`tests/test_repo_layout.py` was 17 s for sixteen paths, one `git grep` each; one `git grep` with all sixteen `-e` regexes took 20 s) | git's extended-regex matcher is the cost, not the tree: the same sixteen names as fixed strings (`-F`) are found in 0.1 s | Find the candidate files with one `git grep -l -F` over the plain text, then run the regex only on those files (`_git_grep` in `tests/test_repo_layout.py`). The same shape helps a Python scan: test a plain substring before a regex `sub` (`_without_exempt_names` in `scripts/check_brand_names.py`), and translate a glob once with `wcmatch.glob.translate` instead of calling `globmatch` per path (`_matcher` in `tests/test_ci_filter_coverage.py`, 9 s to 0.7 s) |
| A backend test "passes" but takes 15 seconds, and it is about a server that cannot be reached | `agent.common.connect_retry` retries a failed connect six times with real backoff (0, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8 s), and the test waits it out | Unless the retries are what the test is about, set them to zero: `monkeypatch.setattr("agent.common.connect_retry.CONNECT_RETRIES", 0)`. `tests/knowledge_layer_tests/test_connect_retry.py` holds the retry policy itself. To find the next one: `pytest --durations=30 --durations-min=1` |
| Every test of a file that starts a local HTTP server spends 0.50 s in teardown (`pytest --durations=0 --durations-min=0.4` shows a column of `0.50s teardown`) | `socketserver`'s `serve_forever` looks for a shutdown request once per `poll_interval`, 0.5 s by default, and `shutdown()` blocks until it does. 58 teardowns and 13 calls did that in the core suite on 2026-11-11, about 35 worker-seconds | Start the thread with `kwargs={"poll_interval": 0.01}`. `tests/test_test_servers_stop_fast.py` fails a server thread without it |
| The backend core suite takes twice as long with `QDRANT_TEST_URL` set as without (228 s against 94 s on 4 cores), and some 200 end-to-end ingest tests each take 0.7 to 1.0 s whatever they ingest | Not the store. `knowledge_layer.ingest.adapter._release_memory` runs `gc.collect()` and `malloc_trim` after every file; in a suite process the collector walks everything the suite has loaded, about 0.28 s a call (`python -m cProfile` on one test shows `gc.collect` on top). Without a server those tests skip, so a local run never showed it | `knowledge_layer_testing.ingest_fixtures` stubs the release for every test under `tests/` (autouse, registered in `tests/conftest.py`). A new suite that ingests end to end registers it too. Time the suite with a Qdrant server running |
| `task be:test:durations` ends with `tests/test_no_legacy_env_names.py` red, naming lines of `tests/.test_durations` | The file is keyed by test id, and a parametrized id quotes its arguments, an old variable name among them. The committed file held 13,280 of 19,120 tests until 2026-11-11, which is how it stayed green | The file is on that test's allow-list now. A new guard that scans every tracked file needs the same entry |
| From a cloud session, a RIS document fetch returns an HTML page titled `Security Check` with status 503, while a search on `data.bka.gv.at` answers JSON (2026-11-11) | The document host (`www.ris.bka.gv.at`) answers automated clients from that network with a WAF challenge; the OGD API host does not. The cluster's fetcher was not checked from here | Do not work around the challenge. Test the lookup offline with the parity rig (`agent_testing.law_lookup_parity`: search answers recorded from the API, document pages in RIS's shape with test text), and measure live answers on dev through the door. Re-record a search answer with `python -m agent_testing.law_lookup_parity`, run from `packages/agent/tests/testing` |
| After `git pull` or a branch switch, backend tests fail at import with `ModuleNotFoundError` for a package the repo itself ships or newly depends on (`public_sources`, `stdnum`) | `uv.lock` moved and `.venv` did not. The session start hook syncs once, at start, not after a later checkout | `task setup`, or `uv sync --group dev` for the backend alone, after any pull that moves a lockfile |
| A Dateien change looks right in `/dev/files-page` and `/dev/file-browser` and wrong on dev: a bordered strip across the whole page („Findings from the documents“), a dashed „Drag files here“ tile among the files, a path row under the tabs, „All files“ over a folder's contents | The previews compose `FileBrowserPane` themselves, and their project had seven loose files, four folders and nothing deeper than two levels; `FileWorkspace` and `ProjectFileWorkspace` add what no preview drew (the findings, the upload tile, the upload tray, the folder's describe line), and a real project has few loose files, many folders and levels a person walks into (dev, 2026-11-10) | Judge Dateien on `/dev/files-page?project=real` and its states (`?folder=r-deep-5`, `r-test`, `r-grund`, `r-berichte&picking=1`, `?folders=many`, `?brief=open`, `?q=`, `?upload=1`; the list is in the page's header comment). Anything you add to `FileWorkspace`'s column, add to that preview in the same change. `bun run clip-check -- --base=http://localhost:<port>` walks those states at five widths |
| A Dateien page in the section frame dies with `Maximum update depth exceeded`, the stack in `ProjectSectionTabActions.useLayoutEffect` | A component that registers something with the frame through a context (`FolderBreadcrumbRow` registers the level's tools) also reads a context the frame rebuilds on every registration: one context object holding both the tools and the tool row's tier re-rendered the registrant, which registered a new node, and so on | Keep what a registrant reads apart from what it writes: the tier (`ToolTierContext`) and the tools (`LevelToolsContext`) are two contexts, and the tier's value is a string, stable across renders |
| A name drawn by `FittedName` (`FileName`) shows only „…“, and its `data-fitted` box measures a few pixels | It measures its own width and fits the name to it; inside a flex row that sizes it by its content (a list row's name line, `flex flex-wrap`), its content is the fitted name, so it measured itself down to the ellipsis | Give it the line's width at the call site (`className="flex-1"`, or a block parent); never let its width follow its own text |
| `bun run clip-check` (or any Playwright script) stops at once with `browserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at /opt/pw-browsers/chromium_headless_shell-<n>/…` | The workspace's `playwright` expects a browser build the image did not download; another build is in `/opt/pw-browsers` | Pass the one that is there: `bun run clip-check -- --base=http://localhost:<port> --chrome=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium_headless_shell-<m>/chrome-headless-shell-linux64/chrome-headless-shell` (`ls /opt/pw-browsers` names it) |
| A sheet's lower edge is missing along part of its length, right above a row that sticks to the top of a scroll box | A sheet's 12% edge is a ring OUTSIDE its box (`0 0 0 1px`), and the sticky row below it paints the page's fill over that pixel: the old path row (`sticky top-0 bg-background`) cut the findings box's lower edge on dev | Leave a gap between a sheet and a band with a fill, or give the band no fill. `bun run clip-check` reports it as `edge-painted-over`; the clipping cousin (an ancestor's `overflow` at the box) is `clipped-edge`, see „A sheet's edge is a ring outside it“ in the rail's rules |
| `task workspace:a11y` (all `/dev` routes) stalls: a route never loads and `curl` to the dev server times out, while `next-server` sits near 0% CPU; `df` shows the disk at 100% | Turbopack's filesystem cache for `next dev` (on by default in Next 16) keeps every compiled route under `apps/workspace/.next/dev/cache`: about 7 GB after 165 preview routes. With the disk full the compile waits forever instead of failing | Free the disk (`rm -rf apps/workspace/.next` is only a cache), then run the audit a shard at a time (`-- --shard 1/4` and so on), clearing `.next` between shards when space is short. CI runs one shard per job, about a quarter of that |
| Playwright or `agent-browser` against a `/dev` route dies with `page.goto: Page crashed` (or `waitForTimeout: Page crashed`) and the console shows `net::ERR_INSUFFICIENT_RESOURCES` on the first chunk, while a blank page still renders and `free` shows gigabytes spare | The disk is full, not the memory: Chromium cannot write its profile and cache, and reports it as a resource error. Several worktrees' `apps/workspace/.next` caches (2–3 GB each) filled the session's allowance. Launch flags (`--disable-dev-shm-usage`, a bigger heap) change nothing | `df -h /` first. Delete the `.next` of worktrees whose dev server is not running (it is only a cache), then retry |
| The **brand-names** hook fails in CI with `<file>: N brand hit(s), baseline 0` for a file you added, though `check_brand_names.py --rebaseline` passed locally a moment earlier | The check measures only what `git ls-files` lists, so a file not yet staged is invisible to `--rebaseline` and the hook passes; CI checks out the commit, sees the file, and counts it | `git add` the new files first, then `--rebaseline` (or `--update`), then `git add` the rewritten baseline too, then commit: a baseline rewritten after the stage is left out of the commit, and CI then fails it the other way (`down from N to M, so lower its baseline entry`). The same holds for `check_file_size.py`. Better still, name the thing for what it does and leave the baseline alone |
| `check_brand_names.py` or `check_brand_prose.py` passes when you run it by hand, and the **brand-names** or **brand-prose** hook then fails at `git commit` with `<file>: N brand hit(s), baseline 0` for a file you just wrote | Both scripts list their files with `git ls-files` (`tracked_files` in each), so an untracked file is not scanned at all. The hand run saw a tree without your new file; `git add` made it tracked, and the hook at commit counted it for the first time | `git add` the new files before you run either check by hand, then fix the hits (write the role noun, "the agent", or `{productName}`; a path that carries an old name counts inside backticks too) and stage again. The same holds for `tests/test_english_names.py` and `check_file_size.py` |
| The **country-in-code** hook fails with `<file>: N country hit(s), baseline 0` (or `baseline M`) on a file that names RIS, the OIB, a Land, `'at'` or an Austrian host | A country is records, never code, and `scripts/check_country_in_code.py` counts the tokens of `ci/country_tokens.json` per source file and per prompt under `apps/` and `packages/` (comments and docstrings left out, strings and file names counted, a prompt counted whole) against `ci/country_in_code_baseline.json` | `.venv/bin/python scripts/check_country_in_code.py --explain <path>` prints each hit with its kind. Read the value from the knowledge context, the pack or the source record instead, and name the thing for what it does; [the burn-down](../architecture/country-extensibility.md#country-in-code-the-burn-down) says what each kind becomes. Sample data belongs in a test, a `*.fixtures.ts` or the gallery, which are not counted. `down from N to M` after a removal: run `--update`. A file moved or renamed: `git add` it, then `--rebaseline`, and review the diff. A quoted `'at'` that is the English word is the one known false positive; `--rebaseline` admits it under review |
| The **brand-names** or **brand-prose** hook fails with `N brand hit(s), baseline 0` for a new test file beside the chat agent's tests (`packages/agent/tests/agents/<chat agent>/`) | Until 2026-11-10 both checks counted every `from agent.agents.<chat agent>… import` line as a hit (the package path keeps the old product name as a wire name, ADR-0149), so a new test of that package could never import from it. Both now exempt the module path on an import line (`IMPORT_PATH` in `scripts/check_brand_names.py` and `check_brand_prose.py`); a hit that remains is the name somewhere else in your file: a path in a docstring (`agents/<chat agent>/x.py`), an imported name, an identifier | Write the path in prose as "the chat agent's `x.py`" and name things for what they do. Do not raise a baseline to admit a new file |
| A model role on `api_type: responses` reads nothing from the prompt cache across turns, though the request carries a block `cache_control` breakpoint in the code (`prompt_caching.cached_system_message`) | The Responses API exposes no per-block `cache_control` (OpenRouter's prompt caching guide), and langchain-openai's Responses converter drops the key: the system block goes out as `input_text` with no marker, and nothing fails. Measured 2026-11-10 | Run the role on Chat Completions (`api_type: chat_completion`, as `research_llm` does since slice Q1). Check the wire, not the message objects: `llm._get_request_payload(prepare_request(llm, messages, kwargs), **kwargs)`, as `test_prompt_caching.py::TestTheMarkerIsOnTheWire` does |
| A public-source fetch from a cloud or CI container gets `HTTP 503 Service Temporarily Unavailable` from `www.ris.bka.gv.at` (a document page, `GeltendeFassung.wxe`), while the OGD search API on `data.bka.gv.at` answers 200 from the same machine | The RIS citizen site refuses this kind of client; the open-data API does not (seen 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-10 from the agent sandbox) | Do not dress the request up as a browser. Prove fetch paths with recorded fixtures (`configs/sources/*/*/fixtures/`, `python -m public_sources test`) and compare live behaviour on the search API only |
| A Landesgesetzblatt amendment's text on RIS names no edition at all (Wien LGBl. 26/2026 never says „OIB-Richtlinie 6") | The authentic gazette (`Applikation=LgblAuth`) ships each Anlage as its own PDF; the XML at `ogd.ris.bka.gv.at/Dokumente/LgblAuth/<ID>/<ID>.xml` holds only the Verordnung's body (seen 2026-11-10) | Read the search result's `Dokumentliste`: each `ContentReference` named „Anlage N" has its PDF URL. In the WBTV 2023, Anlage 13 is OIB-RL 6 |
| An HTML statute parsed with lxml shows `AbstandsflÃ¤chen` for `Abstandsflächen` | `lxml.html.fromstring(bytes)` reads bytes without a declared charset as Latin-1 | `html_statute` takes the record's `encoding` (default `utf-8`) and passes it to the parser; state it in the record rather than sniffing |
| `check_file_size.py` reports dozens of findings (`grew from 1887 to 2020 lines`, `over the 60-line function limit`) in Python files nobody touched, while the pre-commit hook and CI pass | The script was run with the system `python3`. Its `ast` cannot parse the 3.14 syntax the tree is written in, so files and functions are measured wrong; the hook runs `.venv/bin/python` | Run it as the hook does: `.venv/bin/python scripts/check_file_size.py`. Never `--update` or `--rebaseline` from a system interpreter, which writes the wrong measurements into `ci/size_baseline.json`. The script now exits 2 under anything older than 3.14 |
| A failed backend job's log, read through the GitHub log tool or its last lines, holds only Qdrant `actix_web::middleware::logger` lines and container cleanup, and no pytest summary at all | The `qdrant` service container's log is printed after the job's steps; at INFO it wrote one line per request, thousands per suite, and the tool returns the last 5000 lines | The service runs with `QDRANT__LOG_LEVEL: WARN` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; keep it there. On an older run, open the job's pytest step in the browser instead |
| `task setup` fails on a second run with `A virtual environment already exists at: .venv` (`uv venv .venv`), typically right after the session start hook or an earlier setup created the venv | The task created the venv with a separate `uv venv` step, which refuses an existing `.venv`. `uv sync` creates the venv on its own | Fixed in `Taskfile.yml`: `setup` runs `uv sync --group dev` only. If you see it on an older branch, delete that step, not the venv |
| After a directory move (ADR-0145), a spec or test fails with `ENOENT` / `FileNotFoundError` on the OLD path (`…/packages/ris-adapter/src/client.py`), although `git grep -F sources/ris_adapter` came back empty | The path is built from parts, `join(ROOT, 'sources', 'ris_adapter', 'src', 'client.py')` or `ROOT / "sources" / "ris_adapter"`, so no literal search for the old path finds it. Row 6 missed one in a UI spec that way | Search for the old directory names as whole words in every language before pushing a move, not just the joined path: `git grep -nwE 'sources|ris_adapter'`, then read each hit next to a `join(`, `/`, `Path(` or `read(`. Include `apps/workspace/src` even though its noise is high |
| A test fails on your pull request with `FileNotFoundError`/`ENOENT` or a changed constant in a file your change never touched, and it is red on `develop` too | An earlier pull request changed the file that test reads as source (a split, a rename, a moved constant). That pull request touched only the file's own tier, so the test's tier was filtered out and it merged green. #1392 and #1395 were this | Fix the test on its own branch and port it into yours. Then run `python scripts/check_ci_filter_reads.py`: it lists every file a test reads that its tier's filter misses, and `--fix` adds them, so the next change to that file runs the test. `tests/test_ci_filter_reads.py` holds the filters to it in Repo checks |
| A Python test that fakes the BFF fails with `AttributeError: <module 'agent.….client'> has no attribute '_opener'`, or a patched `urllib.request.urlopen` is never called | Every agent-side call to the BFF goes through `agent.common.bff_http`, which owns the one opener; the clients have none of their own, and the opener is not `urlopen` | Patch `bff_http._opener` (the attribute, or its `open`): `monkeypatch.setattr(bff_http._opener, "open", fake)`. `send` reads the module global at call time, so the patch reaches every client. A patch on a client's old name failing loudly is the point: it used to stop reaching the code without a word |
| A test that sets `HTTP_PROXY` sees `bff_http` ignore it, or expects the opener to use a proxy | The BFF opener is a bare `OpenerDirector` with no `ProxyHandler`, on purpose: the service token never travels through an egress proxy. Even a `build_opener` opener reads the proxy environment once, when it is BUILT at import, so a later `setenv` changes nothing | Nothing to fix in the client. A test about proxies rebuilds the opener under its environment: `monkeypatch.setattr(bff_http, "_opener", bff_http._build_opener())` |
| A UI spec passes alone and fails once in a full `verify:fast` run with a value only its own earlier case stubbed (`bff/mail-import/service.spec.ts > stages the archive …` saw `MAIL_IMPORT_MAX_BYTES=100`, "The archive exceeds the maximum of 100 B"), and the re-run is green | Not established. Only that spec writes the variable, and its `beforeEach` calls `vi.unstubAllEnvs()`; the vm pool shares one `process.env` across the spec files a worker runs, so a stub that outlives its case reaches the next one | A case that lowers a limit a later case relies on restores it itself, in a `finally` (`vi.unstubAllEnvs()`, then re-stub what the file's `beforeEach` set), rather than trusting the next `beforeEach`. If it recurs, reproduce with `--maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` over the run's file order and write the cause here |
| A UI spec that never cared about the backend's credential now fails with `InternalServiceUnavailableError: INTERNAL_API_TOKEN is not configured`, or with `Withholding the internal token`, or a catch-all turns either into `Could not reach …` | Every BFF → backend call takes the token from `internalServiceHeaders(baseUrl)`, which sends nothing without a token or to plain HTTP on a public-looking host; the old call sites sent an empty header and the spec's mocked `fetch` never noticed | Give the spec the environment the code needs: `vi.stubEnv('INTERNAL_API_TOKEN', 'test-token')` in a `beforeEach`, with `vi.unstubAllEnvs()` after, and a fake backend that looks internal (`http://backend:8000`, not `http://backend.test`). Do not catch the error in the code to make the spec pass |
| `mcp-server.spec.ts` fails `… and 9000 for any catalog operation` on `project-profile-replace` (or the average tips) after you added an evidence kind to `bff/project-profile/evidence-schema.ts`, though you added no operation | The replace operation's body is the whole profile schema, and the tools-mode export inlines (MCP clients read `$ref` unevenly), so the evidence union is written out four times: facts, assumptions, and each one's alternatives. One more evidence kind of five fields cost about 1,400 characters there (8,386 → 9,774 for `register`, ADR-0172) | Keep a new evidence kind to the fields a person reads (an id that already carries its namespace, `at-bev:5734380`, instead of a separate register field; no `.regex()` where a `max` will do). If it is still over, raise the cap with the measurement and reason beside it, as the spec's comment does |
| `mcp-server.spec.ts` fails `stays under 1000 characters a tool on average` (`expected 1000.0… to be less than 1000`) after you added one reasonable operation | The tools-mode ratchet averages the JSON of EVERY listed tool (backend read tools included), and the catalog's growth has used the headroom it was set with (922 when it was set; 997.4 across 239 tools once the mail operations landed, 987.4 after they were tightened, October 2026). The limit is what tools mode costs a client on every conversation (ADR-0122, "Search and tools mode"). Any new tool over 1000 characters tips it. Measure, don't guess: a temporary `console.log` of `sizes` in the spec lists each tool's size; a discriminated-union body with a `.describe()` on every field is the usual ballast | Trim your own schema `.describe()`s first (an id's source in two words, no describe on a self-explanatory field, `optional()` instead of `nullable()` saves an `anyOf`), then your description: when to call it and what next, nothing the schema or the annotations already say (`idempotent: true` is `idempotentHint`). Keep the words people search with in the `summary` (`search-ranking.spec.ts` looks for "email", not "mail"). Each `z.iso.date()` field carries a leap-year `pattern` of about 250 characters into the JSON Schema: a query with four dates cost 1,000 (S11, 2026-11-10); there a short regex plus a `.refine` on `z.iso.date()` keeps the calendar check off the wire (`queryDay` in `bff/base-knowledge/wire/time.ts`). Raising `MAX_AVERAGE_CHARS` (1,060 since 2026-11-04) is a decision with a reason written beside it, not a fix |
| CI's `tests/test_english_names.py` fails on a new file (`German identifiers added … {'tests/…': (0, 2)}`) that passed the same test locally | The check reads `git ls-files`, so an untracked file is not scanned at all until it is committed | The `english-names` pre-commit hook now runs it on every Python change at commit time (staged files are tracked). Run `tests/test_english_names.py` after `git add`, not before; a German test name such as `test_current_fassung_…` becomes `test_current_revision_…` (glossary: Fassung → revision) |
| `task verify` (`lint:repo` → `agents:audit`) fails with thousands of `- created: …/.claude/worktrees/agent-…/.venv/…` lines and "the deployed skills do not match apm.lock.yaml", or reports hundreds of `critical finding(s) … hidden characters detected` in `.claude/worktree…` (soft hyphens in the worktrees' German docs), although nobody touched `skills/` | Claude Code puts isolated agent worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/`, and `apm audit` treats everything under `.claude/` as generated skill output, so each worktree's files read as drift | Not a repo fault: CI has no worktrees. A test that walks the directory trips on them the same way; scan `git ls-files` instead, as `tests/test_bun_version_pin.py` now does. Run the gate's other tiers directly (`task be:verify workspace:verify site:verify infra:types infra:test`) while agent worktrees exist, or remove them (`git worktree remove`) once their branches are merged, then run `task verify` whole |
| An agent's edits vanish or come back half-merged (a lost import, a reverted class) while several agents edit one checkout | One agent ran `git stash` / `git stash pop` to check a baseline; the stash took every agent's uncommitted edits with it, and the pop conflicted with files written in between | With more than one writer in a checkout, never stash, reset or checkout files you did not write. Read a baseline with `git show HEAD:<path>`, or give each agent its own worktree (`isolation: "worktree"`) |
| Files another agent or a running process is writing disappear from the working tree in the middle of your `git commit` and come back seconds later; the commit output says `[WARNING] Unstaged files detected.`, `Stashing unstaged files to …patch` and at the end `Restored changes from …patch`; a test run or dev server started by someone else fails meanwhile on a file that "does not exist" | pre-commit runs the hooks against the staged content only. When the tree holds unstaged changes it writes them to a patch file, checks every tracked file out as staged (`git checkout -- .`) for as long as the hooks run, and applies the patch afterwards (`pre_commit/staged_files_only.py`). Everyone else's unfinished edits to tracked files are gone for that time, and a file written in between can make the restore conflict | Do not let the hooks run in a checkout another writer is working in. Stage only your finished paths (`git add <paths>`) and commit them from the index without the hooks (`git commit --no-verify`), which stashes nothing. Then check that commit where nobody else writes: in a separate `git worktree` at the commit, run `.venv/bin/pre-commit run --from-ref HEAD~1 --to-ref HEAD` and the tests, and fix forward before you push (a worktree needs its own venv, see the worktree rows above). Or wait until the other writers are done. `git stash` by hand has the same effect and is the row above |
| In a `git worktree`, `git commit` stops with `` `pre-commit` not found.  Did you forget to activate your virtualenv? `` although `.venv/bin/pre-commit` exists in your worktree | Every worktree shares the main checkout's `.git/hooks/pre-commit`, and the script names the Python of whichever checkout last ran `pre-commit install` (`INSTALL_PYTHON=…/worktrees/agent-…/.venv/bin/python`). Once that worktree is removed, the hook falls back to `pre-commit` on the `PATH`, and there is none | Put your own venv first for the commit: `PATH="$PWD/.venv/bin:$PATH" git commit …`. Do not re-run `pre-commit install` from a worktree, which repoints the shared hook at a venv that goes away with it; run it in the main checkout |
| In a `git worktree`, `git commit` fails on `release notes keep to the house rules....Failed` (or another hook that runs `.venv/bin/python`) with no rule named, while `task release:lint` passes | Those hooks run `.venv/bin/python` relative to the worktree, and a worktree has no `.venv`: the hook fails to start, not on your note | Symlink the main checkout's venv into the worktree (`ln -s <main checkout>/.venv .venv`; `.venv/` in `.gitignore` does not match a symlink, so leave it unstaged and remove it when done), or run `task setup` there |
| After `uv run …` in a `git worktree` whose `.venv` is the symlink to the main checkout's, the MAIN checkout's tests run your worktree's code (or, once the worktree is gone, fail to import `agent`); `uv run` printed `Uninstalled 7 packages … Installed 7 packages` | `uv run` syncs the venv to the project it runs in, and the symlinked venv is the main checkout's: it rewrites every editable install (`__editable__.agent-2.0.0.pth` and the workspace packages' finders) to point at the worktree. `uv run python scripts/generate_card_schema.py`, as `AGENTS.md` spells it, does this | In a borrowed venv run `.venv/bin/python …` instead (the card-schema generator runs that way). If it happened, restore from the main checkout with `uv run --frozen python -c pass` there and check `cat .venv/lib/python*/site-packages/__editable__.agent-*.pth` names the main checkout. `uv run --no-project` (the release-note tasks) does not touch the venv |
| In a `git worktree`, a change to `packages/knowledge-layer`, `packages/ris-adapter` or `packages/tavily-web-search` makes no test fail (or a fix makes none pass), while the same change in the main checkout does; a deliberate `raise` in the package changes nothing | The venv's editable installs pin those three packages to the checkout that ran `uv sync`: `__editable___knowledge_layer_1_0_0_finder.py` maps `knowledge_layer` to `<main checkout>/packages/knowledge-layer/src`. Pytest's `pythonpath` (root `pyproject.toml`) puts only this checkout's `packages/agent/src` and `apps/agent-host/src` first, so a worktree that borrows the main venv (the symlink above) runs the agent from the worktree and the packages from the main checkout | Run `task setup` in the worktree so its own venv points at its own packages, or check where an import resolves before you trust a result: `.venv/bin/python -c "import knowledge_layer; print(knowledge_layer.__file__)"`. The agent package is not affected |
| On Windows, `task release:lint` dies with `UnicodeDecodeError: 'charmap' codec can't decode byte 0x9d` after your note already passed the rule checks | Python on Windows opens files in the ANSI code page unless told otherwise, and reno reads the repo's UTF-8 notes and history | Run it with `PYTHONUTF8=1` set; CI's Repo checks job runs on Linux and never sees this |
| CI's **SAST (Semgrep, new findings)** job blocks a PR on `python-logger-credential-disclosure` at a log line that holds nothing secret, such as one that counts tokenizer tokens | The rule fires on words in the format string (`token`, `secret`, `key`, `credential`), not on what is logged. No local hook runs Semgrep, so the first sign is the CI job | When the line logs only names and counts, keep the accurate word and put `# nosemgrep: python.lang.security.audit.logging.logger-credential-leak.python-logger-credential-disclosure` with a one-line reason directly above the call (`stamp_chunk_metadata` in `knowledge_layer/ingest/chunk_metadata.py`, `cross_encoder.py`). When it could log a secret, log less |
| `task workspace:blast-radius` fails (`a new hub, reached by N specs` or `reached by N specs, was M`) on `develop`, or on a pull request that touched nothing near the module it names | Two pull requests each passed against the baseline, and together they widen a module: one added the edge or the specs, the other merged first. The check runs on the merge commit, so the first pull request after both sees it | Run `node scripts/blast-radius.mjs --top 40` in `apps/workspace` and find the edge: the module it names, or the first non-leaf above it, now imports a subtree or is imported by a hub it was not before. Cut that edge when it is a barrel or a hub reaching down. When the growth is inherent (a widely rendered helper gained one more importer), run `task workspace:blast-radius -- --update` and say in the pull request which module grew and why. Never raise `THRESHOLD` or `SLACK` to get green: a leaf is already exempt, and the slack already absorbs suite growth |
| `task workspace:blast-radius` fails with `a new hub` naming a module your change only split or gave one import (`audit/schemas.mjs`, `documents/lifecycle-types.ts` in the mail feature) | A leaf (imports nothing of ours) is exempt, so the module was never in the baseline. Splitting part of it into a second file, or importing one vocabulary into it, makes it a non-leaf at the reach it always had, and everything it now imports inherits that reach | First look for the importer that needs only a sliver: the database schema needed four state tuples of `lifecycle-types.ts`, and `repository/mutations.ts` one string of `filing-choice.ts`, so those moved to leaves (`lifecycle-vocabulary.ts`, `filing-choice-key.ts`) and the hubs stopped reaching the mail modules. When nothing narrower exists (`schemas.mjs` spreads the split-off `schemas-inbound-mail.mjs` into `AUDIT_SCHEMAS`, which `audit/service.ts` needs whole), the count is not growth: add that one module to `scripts/blast-radius.baseline.json` at its count on `develop`, scaled to the baseline's `specs`, rather than `--update`, which also re-records every module that grew within its slack |
| CI's **SAST (Semgrep, new findings)** job blocks a PR on `non-literal-import` at an `importlib.import_module(...)` | The rule fires on any non-literal argument, including a name taken from the module's own literal table | When the name comes from a map in the code (`agent/lazy_exports.py`), put `# nosemgrep: python.lang.security.audit.non-literal-import.non-literal-import` with a one-line reason directly above the call. | `task be:test:sources` is green locally and CI's **Backend suites** job fails in `packages/knowledge-layer/tests/test_qdrant_*.py` (`assert 0 == 1` on a `store.count(...)`, or `FamilyUnreadable`) | Those tests run against a real Qdrant at `QDRANT_TEST_URL` and skip without it, so a local run never executes them. Their fixtures pass payload keys as keyword arguments (`_record(..., punkt_depth=1)`), so renaming such a kwarg changes the stored key the test writes, not a variable | Run them locally before pushing anything under the Python packages (`packages/knowledge-layer`, `ris-adapter`, `tavily-web-search`): start a Qdrant 1.19 server (Docker, or the release binary: `QDRANT__STORAGE__STORAGE_PATH=./qstore ./qdrant`) and run `QDRANT_TEST_URL=http://localhost:6333 .venv/bin/python -m pytest packages/knowledge-layer packages/ris-adapter packages/tavily-web-search -n 8 --dist loadfile` (or `task be:test:sources`). | `task be:verify` fails in a cloud session on `evals/turn-shapes/test_turn_shapes_live.py` (`write_file was never called; trace was []`) while CI is green | The live turn-shape eval ran in the gate whenever `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` was set; a cloud session's environment can carry the key, and its turns reach no model from there. pyproject's `addopts` (`-m 'not live'`) now deselects it everywhere but `task be:eval:turn-shapes`, so this is only seen there | Run the gate with the key unset (`env -u OPENROUTER_API_KEY -u OPENROUTER_KEY task be:verify`); the benchmark runs on demand from `.github/workflows/turn-shapes-live.yml` |

| A profile fact the wizard clearly set reads as missing in code: `profile.facts.bauweise` is `undefined`, a ranking or check ignores the Bauweise, Nutzungen or Geschoße of a real project, while a test with a hand-written profile passes | The intake asks module C and D per building and stores the answers under the instance: `bauweise@bw1`, `nutzungen@bw2`, a use zone's under `x@bw1@wohnen`. | `tsc` or a `pulumi preview` in `deploy/pulumi` fails with `error TS5107: Option 'moduleResolution=node10' is deprecated and will stop functioning in TypeScript 7.0` right after a TypeScript bump | TypeScript 6 turns `"moduleResolution": "node"` (node10) into an error.
