Switching it off is behind the `runtime-model-config` entitlement flag | For a local or test office only: Organization → Models, or `scripts/local-stack/local-stack.sh zdr off`. In a real office the refusal is the control working (the row on `No endpoints found` under Data and correctness) |
| You set `DEFAULT_MODEL` and turns still run on `openai/gpt-6-luna`, or on whatever someone picked in the admin UI | A turn's model comes from the database: the office's own choice (Organization → Models), else `platform_model_defaults`, which the UI fills with `openai/gpt-6-luna` for every agent group on its first boot (`bff/model-config/bootstrap-defaults.ts`) once it can read the backend's config. `DEFAULT_MODEL` applies only to a group with neither, and to `summary_llm` and `rerank_llm` | Change the model where it is stored: Platform → Models for the fleet, Organization → Models for one office (`scripts/local-stack/local-stack.sh model <id>` sends the same request). [`org-model-configuration.md`](../architecture/org-model-configuration.md) |
| `checkpoints.db`, `jobs.db` and `summaries.db` (with `-shm`/`-wal`) appear in the repository root after a local `nat serve`; `git status` does not show them | `configs/config_oib_openrouter.yml` defaults `CHECKPOINT_DB` to `./checkpoints.db` and `NAT_JOB_STORE_DB_URL` / `SUMMARY_DB` to `sqlite+aiosqlite:///./…db`, relative to the working directory, and `.gitignore`'s `*.db` hides them. Two checkouts or two stacks then share or clobber one store | Point the three variables outside the checkout, as `scripts/local-stack/local-stack.sh` does (`$LOCAL_STACK_DIR/backend/`). Delete the stray files once nothing runs on them |
| Playwright's `page.goto(url, { waitUntil: 'networkidle' })` times out on every `/app` page, though the page has rendered | The page keeps reconnecting to `/api/stream` (the collaboration event hub, `ui/features/collaboration/lib/event-hub.ts`) with a backoff for as long as it is open; with the `collaboration` flag off every attempt answers 403. The network is never idle | `waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded'`, then wait for the control you need (`scripts/local-stack/smoke-memory.cjs`) |

## Data and correctness

| Symptom | Cause | Do this |
|---|---|---|
| Two tiers disagree about one upload: the BFF hashes `innocent.pdf` as one file and the api tier extracts it as a ZIP, or a ZIP member `x.pdf\0y.pdf` is one name to fflate and `x.pdf` to Python (found in review of #1608, both reproduced) | The part's `Content-Type` is the sender's word, and Python's `zipfile.ZipInfo.filename` is already cut at the first NUL (`orig_filename` keeps the archive's spelling) while fflate keeps the NUL; for one member path held twice, `zipfile` iterates both and our reader keeps the first, while `unzipSync` returns an object and so the last. A guard in a tier that does not write, on its own parse of the upload, guards its own reading only | Put the rule where the write happens (`oib_sync.store_and_request` refuses other bytes under a name it keeps), decide the format by the bytes in both tiers (`ZIP_MAGIC` in `agent_host/corpus_upload.py` and `bff/base-knowledge/platform/upload-hashes.ts`), read `orig_filename` before you judge a member's name, and refuse a ZIP with one path twice in both (count paths in fflate's `filter`, which sees every entry). The same `filter` is where to bound a ZIP's unpacked size: `unzipSync` allocates each member's declared `originalSize`, so sum it there and return false past the limit |
| A CHECK meant to say "this column is one of these values, and only on rows of that kind" (`CHECK (origin IN ('a','b') AND basis = 'fetched')`) refuses every row of the other kind although their column is NULL | `NULL AND false` is `false`, not NULL: the IN yields NULL for a NULL column and the second condition is false on the other rows, so the CHECK fails where you expected it not to apply | Say the NULL case first: `CHECK (origin IS NULL OR (origin IN (…) AND basis = 'fetched'))`, and compare a nullable boolean through `coalesce(…, false)` (migration 0224). Seed a row of each kind in `scripts/rls-test-db.sh` before the migration applies, so the ALTER meets them. And ask what the previous build writes: a CHECK that requires a new column refuses the old pods' inserts during a rollout and after a rollback, so 0224 treats a missing value as untrusted instead of refusing it |
| An integration spec proposes a `source` record and the proposal fails KC-1 at `/territoryId` (`names territory AT, which is not imported`), or the proposal passes and the later submit fails `1 check(s)` with no source problem in sight | A source lies in a territory of the request's country (an ISO-style id; `CC:scheme:code` is refused for a source), which the throwaway database holds only if a suite proposed it. And submit runs KC-7 again, which asks the api tier (`POST /v1/public-sources/check`): a `fetch` stub taken down after the proposal leaves it unanswered, and an unanswered check is a problem | Propose a territory of the suite's own in the same batch (`AT-TXS` in `texts.integration.spec.ts`, its name quoted from the capture for KC-6) and keep the api-tier stand-in up until the case ends (`apiTier` there) |
| A migration's `WITH RECURSIVE` fails with `recursive reference to query "…" must not appear within its non-recursive term`, or you need two kinds of step (edition to work, and back along a relation) | PostgreSQL allows exactly one recursive term; `base UNION step1 UNION step2` reads step1 as part of the non-recursive term | Put every step in ONE recursive term: `SELECT step.* FROM ends CROSS JOIN LATERAL (SELECT … UNION ALL SELECT …) step`, each branch reading `ends` columns only (`knowledge_reached_by_relation` in migration 0211) |
| An integration spec's `rejects.toThrow(/my trigger's message/)` fails although the trigger fired: the error reads `Failed query: insert into …` | Drizzle wraps the driver's error; the trigger's `RAISE` text is on the error's `cause`, not its `message` | Match the cause: `rejects.toMatchObject({ cause: expect.objectContaining({ message: expect.stringMatching(/…/) }) })` (`chain/chain.integration.spec.ts`) |
| A mark you set on a base-corpus file's points (`PUT …/knowledge-mark`) is gone after an admin re-ingests the file | `oib_sync` builds the base corpus's ingest job from the file and its Dokumentart only, so the rebuilt points carry no mark; an office copy's job carries its mark (`KnowledgeMarkFields`), a base-corpus file's does not | Before relying on a payload field for base-corpus points, make the corpus row and its ingest job carry it. Until then map a base-corpus passage to its edition through the records (the manifestation's `corpus_file`, `citations/roles.py`), as ADR-0168 does |
| Two jsonb objects that hold the same fields compare unequal after a round trip (`JSON.stringify(stored) !== JSON.stringify(parsed)`), so a check "is this entry already there" says no for an entry that is | Postgres `jsonb` stores an object's keys in its own order (shorter keys first), and zod writes them in schema order; `JSON.stringify` keeps whichever order it was given | Compare a tuple of the fields that identify the entry, as `addsRegisterEvidence` does (`apps/workspace/bff/project-profile/register-evidence.ts`), never the serialized object |
| A bulk insert through postgres-js (`` tx`INSERT INTO t ${tx(rows)}` ``) passes on a fixture of twenty rows and is refused on the real import, once one batch carries more than 65,535 values (found by arithmetic before the first full import, ADR-0172, not by the error) | One statement takes at most 65,535 bind parameters, and a multi-row insert spends one per column per row: 5,000 rows of 14 columns is 70,000. A small fixture never reaches it, so only the real import does | Size the batch from the columns (`IMPORT_BATCH_SIZE` in `bff/site-resolution/import.ts` is 4,000 for 14), and hold it with a spec that multiplies the two (`resolution.spec.ts`), so adding a column fails a test instead of the next import |
| A refused insert inside a platform transaction is caught (`isUniqueViolation`) and the run still fails with `PostgresError: duplicate key value violates unique constraint …`, when the savepoint around it is raw SQL (`tx.execute(sql\`SAVEPOINT x\`)` … `ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT x`) | Seen on the source monitor's one-open-request index (2026-11-10, ADR-0174): the raw savepoint did not hold the failed statement's effect on the drizzle transaction; the same insert inside `tx.transaction(async (savepoint) => …)` was caught and the run went on | Take a savepoint with drizzle's nested `tx.transaction`, never with hand-written `SAVEPOINT` statements (`openRequest` in `bff/base-knowledge/platform/monitor.ts`) |
| A new record type is proposed and written, but its revision has no claims in `change-request-get`, so no reviewer verdict can name one and the approval trigger refuses the request | The platform store kept its own list of revision body tables beside `BODY_TABLES` in `store/repository.ts`, and the new type was added to one list only (found 2026-11-10 adding `source`) | There is one list now: `BODY_TABLES` in `bff/base-knowledge/store/repository.ts`, which `platform/store.ts` walks. A new record type adds its body table there |
| A search, workspace search, cross-project search or „Im Büro behandelt“ answers **400** (`A read over the office scopes [...] needs the office it reads as`), or a single-collection search returns nothing and logs a failed retrieval | Since ADR-0163 a Qdrant read over an office's own scopes (`proj_`, `s_`, the office shelf) cannot be built without the office it reads as (`vector_store.ScopeRead`), and the agent-host search routes take that office from the signed request envelope's `organizationId`. A BFF call that signs only `{ collectionScope }` gives them none; an office shelf alone still works because its name carries the organization | Sign the office with the scope: `buildRequestContextWireHeaders({ collectionScope, organizationId }, …)`, as `documents/semantic-search.ts` and `similar-passages/service.ts` do. Inside the agent the request context carries it; a plain FastAPI route wraps its search in `knowledge_layer.search.reading_as(office)` |
| A repeated proposal adds a revision every time instead of answering `unchanged`, though the record is identical; a comparison of a stored jsonb object with the one just parsed is always false | jsonb keeps its own key order (shorter keys first, then by bytes), not the order the object was written in, so `JSON.stringify` of the value read back differs from the zod-parsed record's | Compare canonically, keys sorted at every level (`proposalText` in `bff/base-knowledge/platform/store.ts`). Only an integration spec sees it: a mocked store hands back the object it was given. `onboarding-door.integration.spec.ts` ("changes nothing when it is sent again") holds it |
| A German, Swiss or „other" project is answered with OIB verdicts, Austrian doctrine or a Gebäudeklasse; a project with no Land is treated as Austrian | `resolve_country` matched `country=([a-z]{2})\b`, so `country=other` never matched (no word boundary after `ot`) and fell through to the default `at`; `de` and `ch` matched but had no profile, and every caller fell back to Austria. The OIB block had no country gate at all (design `docs/design/international/design.md` 2.2) | Closed for good (ADR-0175): the agent reads no country from the project's text at all. The knowledge context the BFF resolves is the only source (`common/knowledge_context.py`, `common/law_gate.py`), and a turn or run without one is `knowledge_unavailable`. Never default a project's country, and never read `organizations.home_country` as one. `test_turn_context_message.py` and `test_knowledge_context.py` hold it |
| A DXF renders without its parcel numbers or symbols, or ezdxf logs `Deleted entity INSERT(#…) without required BLOCK definition`; the file came from BEV ("BEV-DXF+CSV") or another tool that writes partial R12 files | The file has no BLOCKS section and expects the receiving system to hold the symbol library (BEV's DXF spec v2.6, §1). ezdxf's `audit()`, and `ezdxf.recover`, which audits, delete every INSERT whose block is missing, attributes and all, without raising | Read with `ezdxf.readfile` (no audit), define an empty stand-in for every missing block name, and only then audit or draw: `define_missing_blocks` in `apps/agent-host/src/agent_host/drawings/dxf_source.py`. `test_a_header_less_bev_file_keeps_every_parcel_number` holds it. Never fall back to `recover` first |
| A DXF rendered through ezdxf's matplotlib backend has hairline lines everywhere: a 0.35 mm boundary prints at about 0.12 mm | ezdxf hands matplotlib each line weight in millimetres, and matplotlib reads a line width as points (1 pt = 0.353 mm) | Set `lineweight_scaling=72/25.4` and `min_lineweight` in points on the drawing `Configuration` (`_draw_model` in `apps/agent-host/src/agent_host/drawings/dxf_pdf.py`). `test_line_weights_plot_as_they_say` holds it |
| Rendering a DXF to PDF takes minutes once it has thousands of texts or hatches, and a profile shows `Axes._update_patch_limits` and `bezier.axis_aligned_extrema` on top | `Axes.add_patch` recomputes the data limits from every path's Bézier extrema, and ezdxf adds each glyph outline as a patch: 3,600 parcels took 121 s, 18 of every 22 seconds there | When the limits are set by you (a sheet at a scale), add patches with `add_artist`, which skips that work (`_FixedLimitsBackend` in `dxf_pdf.py`), and set plain labels as PDF text rather than outlines (`text_layer.py`). `test_a_whole_cadastral_community_renders_inside_the_budget` holds it |
| A compare-and-set on a `timestamptz` column (`WHERE reader_fault_at = $seen`) never matches, though the row was just read and nobody touched it; the sweep counts `gone` for every row | Postgres keeps microseconds, a JS `Date` milliseconds: the value read back (`12:38:57.857705`) becomes `…57.857Z`, and equality against it is false | Compare at the precision you can hold: `date_trunc('milliseconds', col) = $seen` (`faultSeen` in `lib/inbound-mail/sweep-repository.ts`), or CAS on a column you write yourself (a uuid, a counter). Only an integration spec sees it: a mocked repository compares the `Date` it was given |
| A bulk upload fails a burst of files 1–6 minutes after upload, the `document_ingest_finished` events carry no reason, Langfuse shows almost no `ingest-file` errors, and the files read fine when retried later; the worker log has `Download of the original failed: ConnectError` and the BFF logs `[db] database unavailable` in the same minutes (2026-11-02: 109 of 556 files) | Prod runs SeaweedFS as one `weed server` pod (`seaweedfsTopology: single`) with the pre-ADR-0043 limit of 1 GiB (`constants.ts`, `seaweedfs`). Eight ingest pods downloading at once took its working set from 226 MB to 1,062 MB and it restarted five times in 15 minutes; it restarts about four times a day even idle. Every download in those seconds fails the file (transient), and the sweep retries it 5 and 10 minutes later | Read `k8s.container.restarts` and `container.memory.working_set` for `seaweedfs-0` in PostHog (`posthog.metrics`) before debugging the pipeline. The fix is the object store's memory, or the split topology (ADR-0043, runbook in `docs/deployment/kubernetes.md`), not a longer ingest timeout |
| `document_ingest_finished` reports hours (often a round-looking number like six) for documents that were read in seconds, and many arrive in the same second | `duration_ms` is measured from the dispatch write to the first read that reconciles the row. Nothing pushes status, so with no tab open and the batch outside the sweep's 25 newest, the event waits for the next person to open a list or ask the agent | Use the `ingest-file` trace latency for processing time. Do not alert on `duration_ms` |
| Hundreds of documents from one bulk upload sit at `pending` for days: `ingest_attempts = 1`, no `ingest_failure_category`, `updated_at` equal to the upload time, no row in `ingest_job_queue`, the batch sealed but never completed, while `ingest-worker` claims normally (found 2026-10-28: batches of 309 and 219 documents) | The BFF asked `POST /v1/documents/status/batch` for every in-flight job id in one call, and the route refuses more than `BATCH_STATUS_MAX_IDS` (200) with a 422. `fetchJson` turns any non-OK answer into `null`, and `null` means "backend unreachable, leave the rows alone", so a batch past the limit was never reconciled by a read or by the sweep, however its jobs ended. The jobs had run: `mark_done` deletes a finished job's queue row, which is why the queue looked empty. Batches of 200 or fewer were unaffected, which is why nothing smaller ever showed it | Fixed by `fetchInChunks` (`bff/documents/status-batch.ts`); `status-batch-contract.spec.ts` pins the BFF's limit to the route's. For rows already stuck, run the catch-up sweep in [`stuck-pending-documents-runbook.md`](../deployment/stuck-pending-documents-runbook.md). A job the backend no longer knows (its status is kept 24 hours) with no indexed file settles `failed`, retryable, once the row is 48 hours old (`bff/documents/lost-job.ts`). A fail-open seam that cannot tell "refused" from "unreachable" hides its own bug: log the refusal, and test it with a list longer than the limit |
| A document a person stopped goes back to being read: its `ingest_jobs` row is `cancelled`, within 30 s `processing` again with the worker as `owner`, and `cancelled` once more seconds to minutes later; a re-ingest started in between joins the job that is about to stop. Or a stopped job ends `failed` with raw exception text | The cancel clears the row's `owner`, like every settle. The worker, still inside a long step and not yet at its next ownership check, beats, refreshes no row, and concludes another replica settled its live job wrongly, so it writes the job back (`ingest_status_store`: the owner, while it lives, is right about its job). Nothing in the row says a person stopped it; only the run knows (its claim guard, `ctx.is_cancelled`) | Fixed in the adapter's `_persist`: a run told to stop writes no live or failed status again, only `completed`. If you still see it, the write landed between the cancel's settle and the cancel reaching the worker (sub-second); `ingest_workflow._stopped` settles the row again, and a worker that dies first leaves it `interrupted` after 120 s, since the cancel also releases `held_until`. Do not make `put` conditional: `completed` must overwrite `cancelled`, and an outsider's `interrupted` must stay overwritable ([Stopping a read](../technical-reference/document-ingestion.md#stopping-a-read-cancel)) |
| `document_ingest_finished` shows `duration_ms` of hours or days for documents that indexed in a minute (feld72, 2026-11-02: 59 PDFs at about six hours; 2026-10-28: 528 at 1.4 to 9.2 days), or a bulk upload's `failed` events carry no `$exception` and no reason | The backend pushes no result into Postgres. A row leaves `pending` only when a read asks the backend: an open tab, or the upload sweep, which covers the 25 newest open batches of the last 7 days. A document in neither waited for the next person to open its shelf, and `duration_ms` is the time until that read (`now - updated_at`), not the worker's run. The event also never carried a reason, and a failure the backend settles without raising (a lost owner, `interrupted`) has no exception anywhere | `reconcileStalePending` (`bff/documents/stale-pending.ts`) reads back every `pending` row older than 5 minutes on the scheduler's 30 s tick. `document_ingest_finished` now carries `error_reason`, `error_category`, `retryable`, `attempt`, and a failed row raises `IngestFailedError` with its organization and document; `document_ingest_started` marks the dispatch. Judge a stuck document by a start with no finish, not by `duration_ms` |
| The worker log warns: `llama-index-readers-file` package not found, some file readers will not be available if not provided by the `file_extractor` parameter. Seen 4× in PostHog (grid-ingest-worker, 2026-10-31) | `adapter.py` (`_run_ingestion`, the last branch) hands a file that is not a PDF, image, rendition, office or text format to `SimpleDirectoryReader`. Its `load_file` calls `supported_suffix_fn()` once per file (`llama_index/core/readers/file/base.py:591`), and that probe logs the warning on every call while the optional package is absent. It is per file, not per start-up, so four warnings mean four such files were read. `file_extractor` does not skip the probe. The types that reach this branch (`.html`, `.json`, or an extension an operator adds to `FILE_UPLOAD_ACCEPTED_TYPES`) are in no default reader, so they are decoded as plain UTF-8 with the package installed or not. Checked on 2026-11-03: the same output with and without it | Nothing is unreadable because of it, and installing `llama-index-readers-file` would change nothing for those types. It would change an extension an operator adds that has a reader there: `.epub` would go through `EpubReader` instead of being decoded as text. Its readers (`.xls`, `.hwp`, `.epub`, `.mbox`, `.ipynb`, audio and video) are reachable only by a type nobody lists. To remove the warning, stop calling `SimpleDirectoryReader` in that branch, and replicate its metadata (`file_path`, `file_type`, the excluded embed keys) first. `tests/knowledge_layer_tests/test_retrieval_scoring_and_identity.py` depends on those keys |
| `tests/db/migrations-journal.test.ts` fails `numbers migrations uniquely and without gaps`, or two open PRs both add migration `02NN` | Each branch's journal must be gapless from develop, so parallel PRs that each add a migration all take the same next number; the second to merge collides | Take the next free number on your branch, say in the PR which other open PR holds the same one, and renumber (file names, `_journal.json` `idx` and `tag`, the `rls-test-db.sh` block, comments) when you merge develop after the other lands. A schema change that removes or narrows is expand/contract (`ci/migration_contract.py`): add the new shape and keep writing the old one, drop it in a later contract migration |
| A law is missing from a turn's knowledge context on the last day of its edition, while `editions/as-of` names that edition for the same day; or a proposed edition overlaps its successor by exactly one day | Until 2026-11-10 an edition's `in_force_to` was the last day in force (the reading RIS prints: the Bauproduktegesetz 1997's `Ausserkrafttretensdatum` is `2018-12-31`, and its successor starts `2019-01-01`), while the chain resolver read every interval half-open, so it dropped the law on that day. "Which edition on a day" was written three times with two end rules | Closed by ADR-0171's amendment and migration 0217: `in_force_to` is the first day no longer in force, every interval in base knowledge is half-open, and `time/editions.ts` `editionInForce` is the one rule the chain asks through `editionFor`. Enter a source's printed last day one day later. `resolve.spec.ts` ("binds a law on its edition’s last day…") and the `dates_ordered` CHECK hold it |
| `task db:test:rls` fails `site-resolution.integration.spec.ts` (`no_territory`, `no_rule`, `ambiguous_rules`): the Graz address has a chain or an authority it should not have; the case passes alone, and a rerun does not help | Integration files share one database. `platform.integration.spec.ts` published the real Graz id (`AT:gkz:60101`) and a competence ruleset on `AT-6`/`AT-9`, so site resolution found a territory and a rule it expected to be absent. Not a deadlock and not timing: pollution, deterministic once both have run (found 2026-11-10, the third "passes alone, fails together" that day) | Closed for this pair: the platform spec publishes run-scoped places (`AT:testgraz:<run>`) and keys its rules on them. A spec that publishes platform records uses run-scoped ids, never a real Gemeinde, Land rule or code another suite reads |
| A follow-up („und für Wohnungen?") is answered as if nothing had been asked before („aus dem Gespräch nicht ersichtlich"), after a turn that read a lot, while a follow-up of a short turn („Danke" after „Hallo") works | The history a model sees is trimmed to `max_history_tokens` keeping the NEWEST messages and starting on a human turn. The previous turn's tool results ride along whole and are the newest messages before the follow-up, so when they overran the budget the trim kept only part of them, and `start_on="human"` then dropped them and the previous question and answer too | Build the window with `history.history_window`, which drops the earlier tool results before the earlier prose. Never call `trim_message_history` on a transcript that still holds tool results; `test_history.py::TestHistoryWindow` fails a window that loses the question it follows |
| A PostHog insight alert shows „Errored" on every hourly check with only „This alert encountered an error. Check the alert configuration and try again.", while its SQL runs fine in PostHog's SQL editor and `alert-simulate` refuses to dry-run it | An `any_row` HogQL alert over a result with more than one row. The support alert, one row, worked; the cost (3 rows) and health (23 rows) alerts errored from creation until they were folded into one row on 2026-10-27. Not the saved views or `posthog.metrics`, which were the first suspects | Return exactly one row: `rule` joining every fired rule, `breach` counting them, with the wrapper in [`deploy/posthog/README.md`](../../deploy/posthog/README.md#alerts). Reported to PostHog through agent feedback |
| A day of Claude chat costs several times what the same day on an OpenAI model did; `llm_usage_hourly` shows `cached_tokens` 0 for every `anthropic/*` row, while the OpenAI rows sit around 50 % | Claude caches nothing unless the request asks, and `prompt_caching.py` sent only the sticky-routing fields because "caching is automatic", which is true of the OpenAI family alone. 60 M Claude prompt tokens in 30 days, none cached, until 2026-10-27 | Closed: `prompt_caching.with_cache_marker` adds a top-level `cache_control` for `anthropic/*`; `test_a_claude_call_asks_for_caching_on_both_apis` holds it on the wire for both APIs, and „Costs and budgets (prod)" fires when the Claude cache share falls under 20 %. A new model family: check its provider's caching docs before trusting the cache, and add it to `EXPLICIT_CACHE_FAMILIES` if it is opt-in |
| After the Zod 4 move a PUT that sets only some agent groups (`/api/platform/reasoning-efforts`, `/api/platform/model-defaults`, `/api/organization/model-config`) is refused with `Invalid input: expected …, received undefined` for every group it left out | Zod 4's `z.record(z.enum([...]), v)` is exhaustive: every enum key must be present. Zod 3's was partial | `z.partialRecord(z.enum([...]), v)` for a map where a key may be left out; its values type as possibly `undefined`, so read them through `definedValues` (`lib/utils/defined-values.ts`) rather than asserting them away |
| A route answers `400 Invalid request body` with `"message": "Invalid UUID"` for an id like `11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111` that passed before the Zod 4 move, and a spec full of such ids fails | Zod 4's `z.uuid()` (and `z.string().uuid()`) checks RFC 9562: a version digit 1-8 and a variant of 8, 9, a or b. Zod 3 checked only the 8-4-4-4-12 shape | The repo uses `z.guid()`, Zod 4's name for Zod 3's check, for every id: every id we mint is a valid UUID, but nothing promised that of an id already stored or of a test fixture. Write `z.guid()`, not `z.uuid()`, for a new id field; tightening is a separate decision |
| A refusal an agent or a test reads changed wording after the Zod 4 move: `Expected number, received string` is now `Invalid input: expected number, received string`, `Required` is `Invalid input: expected string, received undefined` | Zod 4 rewrote its default issue messages; the issue codes and paths are unchanged | Assert on `issue.code` and `issue.path` where the wording is not the point; where it is (a refusal text a spec pins), update the expected string. No error map restores the old texts, and none should |
| A JSON Schema fixture (`tests/fixtures/*.schema.json`, `openapi.json`) gains `minimum`, `minItems`, `pattern` or `default` it never had, with no schema change of yours | Since the Zod 4 move the export is `z.toJSONSchema` (`lib/api/zod-json-schema.ts`), which writes every check zod enforces. The hand-written converter before it dropped numeric bounds, array minimums, `.regex()` patterns and defaults silently | Nothing to fix: the fixture now says what the route already enforced. The exporter still drops on purpose the `pattern` of a `uuid`/`date-time`/`email` format and the default safe-integer bounds of `.int()`; its header says why |
| An integration spec's `expect(...).rejects.toThrow(/permission denied/)` (or a constraint name) fails with `got 'Failed query: DELETE FROM …'`, although the database refused exactly as expected | drizzle wraps every driver error in its own `Failed query: <sql> params: …` error; the PostgresError with the message, `code` and `constraint_name` is its `cause`. Seen in `mail-thread/append.integration.spec.ts` asserting the revoked DELETE | Assert on the cause: `const refused = await run().catch((error: unknown) => error)` then `expect(String((refused as { cause?: unknown }).cause)).toMatch(/permission denied/)`, or match `{ cause: { code: '42501' } }`.
