Without `accepts` the page sends no `cancel_turn.shown` | Unset `WIRE_V2_ADDITIVE_FIELDS` on the backend, or set it `on`, unless the rollback is deliberate. A server-to-client field added while a strict bundle can still be open goes through the same list: [`chat-wire-v2.md`](../design/chat-wire-v2.md#compatibility-additive-changes-are-safe) |
| The page stutters or freezes while an answer streams, worse for users with long chat history; a CPU profile is dominated by `setItem`, the composer or the sessions panel | Every delta flush replaces the open conversation, so anything subscribed to `currentConversation` / `conversations` re-renders with each one, and the persisted store (partialized to the whole history, one key until 2026-09) serializes and writes itself on each one | Select the field you show (`s.currentConversation?.id`), never the conversation; the persisted store never writes a streaming answer's growth (a reload drops it anyway), so a new write path must go through it. Measure on `/dev/stream-socket`; `resilient-storage.spec.ts` fails if the live turn's growth is written again |
| A phone stutters while a turn is working even though nothing is arriving, and the trace shows a `Paint` every frame | An infinite CSS animation of a property no compositor can run: `background-position` (the old label shimmer), `stroke-dashoffset` (React Flow's `animated` edges). Each repaints on the main thread every frame for the whole turn: at rest a live turn cost 262 ms of main thread per second on a 4× throttled CPU, 237 paints | Animate only `opacity` and `transform` in anything that loops, and never animate a property on an element something else already animates: an opacity loop on a label that framer-motion cross-fades made the label flicker at every step (the CSS animation takes the value back the frame framer finishes). `ShimmerText` moves by transform alone for that reason. Nor may a single SVG connector loop: one short marching React Flow edge still ran the whole frame pipeline 60 times a second, 245 ms/s of main thread on a 4× phone at rest, off-screen too. The live Herleitung is the one accepted exception: React Flow's built-in `animated` flag, on the connectors into its newest row only and only while the turn is live (`renderedEdges` in `ReasoningFlow.tsx`), so a settled turn pays nothing. List what runs with `document.getAnimations()` and measure a page at rest with a trace (count `Paint` per second): `/dev/chat-turn` went to 82 ms and 2 paints per second. The other ways to move something ALONG a connector are no better: an SVG `<animateMotion>` dot (≈47 ms/s on a minimal page) and an HTML dot on CSS `offset-path`/`offset-distance` (≈41 ms/s) both run on the main thread; an HTML dot on a `transform` animation cost ≈1 ms/s. The Herleitung once shipped such a dot and went back to the library's own animated edge for simplicity |
| A streaming perf fix measures clean in a store-level harness but the real turn still pins the CPU | A store-level harness (`/dev/stream-replay`) calls the fold directly and gives the turn no steps, so it sees nothing of what the socket carries. On the old wire that was hundreds of 16 to 33 KB step frames per turn (the runtime's stock step adaptor), which is how a turn that held the main thread for 47 s went unnoticed | Measure on `/dev/stream-socket`, which plays a v2 turn through a stubbed `WebSocket` into the real socket client, hook, store and components. `node scripts/measure-stream-socket.mjs --url <dev server>` prints long-task ms, backlog, the largest frame and first-card time ([testing-and-verification.md](testing-and-verification.md#the-socket-level-streaming-harness)) |
| A `/dev` chat harness shows an empty thread or sidebar although it seeds conversations, or its numbers do not change with `history=` | The seeded `userId` is not `'default-user'`. `useWebSocketChat` calls `setCurrentUser('default-user')` on mount (the no-backend identity, `adapters/auth/use-auth.ts`), and the cross-user guard in `sessions-store.ts` then nulls the open conversation and the sidebar filters the rest out. The deleted `/dev/stream-chat` seeded `'dev-user'`, so every measurement it took with `history=40` before 2026-09 was of an empty thread | Seed `'default-user'` in any harness that mounts the chat, as `/dev/chat-welcome` and `/dev/stream-socket` do. Check the thread actually renders its messages before trusting a number |
| Every stored chat is gone after a reload (the stored value a few hundred characters), or a storage harness never reaches the quota however much history it seeds | Until 2026-09 the whole history was ONE localStorage key, and past the quota the recovery wiped every session. A harness that seeds bare answers under-weighs them: forty conversations are 1.4 M characters, a third of what answers with their cards and citations weigh, and Chromium accepted 4.6 M characters in one key and refused 6.8 M | Storage is one key per conversation now and evicts the oldest conversations' messages, which the server holds (`stores/chat-storage.ts`). Seed answers with their cards and citations, and check `readStoredChat` rather than the old key |
| You changed the prompt in Langfuse but the agent still uses the old text | Four candidates, in the order they bite. (1) The change is a new VERSION but the `production` label still points at the old one — the label is what ships, not the save. (2) The fleet is inside its cache window: `LANGFUSE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (60s default), and the SDK serves the stale text while it refreshes, so the first turn after a change is expected to be old. (3) The deployment is on another label (`LANGFUSE_PROMPT_LABEL`) or prompt management is off entirely (`LANGFUSE_PROMPTS_ENABLED` unset) and it is rendering the bundled `chat_static.md`. (4) Langfuse is unreachable, and the store muted it for the TTL window after one failed fetch — the agent log says so once. | Look at the trace, not at the prompt: a generation carries `langfuse.observation.prompt.name` and `.version` only when a LANGFUSE version served — that link names a prompt Langfuse holds, and nothing else may wear it. The bundled fallback is in the trace METADATA instead, as `prompt_name` (`git:prompts/chat_static.md`) and `prompt_version` (the file's git blob hash), so no prompt link at all on the generations is itself the answer: the fallback served. `task prompts:pull -- --label production` prints and writes what Langfuse is actually serving, which settles (1) and (3) in one command. A repo edit to `chat_static.md` reaches a Langfuse-served fleet only through `task prompts:push -- --label <label> --apply` (next row) |
| You changed `chat_static.md`, the image with it deployed, and the agent still renders the old text (or code that depends on the new wording misbehaves) | Prompt management is on for that deployment (`LANGFUSE_PROMPTS_ENABLED`), so the fleet renders what its Langfuse label holds, and the committed file is only the fallback. Git is the source of truth (ADR-0060 (a)) but nothing publishes it on its own | `task prompts:push -- --label <label>` shows the diff; `-- --apply` publishes it. If it refuses, the label holds an edit made in Langfuse: `task prompts:pull -- --label <label>`, review and commit it, then push |
| An answer-suite run shows turns that crashed after the answer streamed (`Bei der Recherche … ein Fehler`), or medians that match neither the old code nor the new | The suite runs every question in its own process from the WORKING TREE, and modules imported lazily are read when first used. Editing the code while it runs measures two codebases, and a signature changed in one file but not yet in its caller crashes the runs caught in between | Measure a committed tree: commit first, or run from a `git worktree` (since `census.tree_pythonpath`, each run imports the worktree's own `src/` and workspace-package code, and the suite refuses to start when one would import another checkout; before that, a worktree without its own venv measured the MAIN checkout under the worktree's commit) with `SUMMARY_DB` pointing at the ingest's database by ABSOLUTE path (the default `./summaries.db` is relative, so a worktree reads an empty inventory: no documents, no family overviews, no quote checks, and 7 s more per turn; the suite now refuses to start on an empty one). The report's header names the commit, `-dirty` when it was not clean, and the suite warns at start |
| Traces have CHAIN and TOOL observations but no GENERATION, while the trace's own `usage_llm_calls` still counts them | An attribute that only generation spans carry held a value Langfuse's ingestion schema rejects. Ingestion is per event, so only the generation events go missing and every other observation arrives: the trace looks healthy and the LLM calls are simply absent, with wall-clock gaps where they ran. That the server drops rather than nulls is read off the exports, not off its logs; if generations stay missing after the fix deploys, the Langfuse web/worker logs around one trace id are the next place to look. Here it was `promptVersion` — emitted as a string where `CreateGenerationBody` declares `Optional[int]`, and as a 7-character git blob hash whenever the bundled prompt served | The version is an int (`prompt_observation_attributes`), and a value that is not one yields no link. A fallback is never linked at all — it names no prompt Langfuse holds, which is why its own SDK links none. `test_prompt_link.py` reads the declared type off the installed SDK, so a Langfuse release that moves it fails the suite instead of the fleet. For a NEW generation-only attribute, check it against the SDK's body model before shipping it: nothing else reports the drop |
| A predicate negated with drizzle's `not(…)` leaves out rows it should keep, and only rows where some column is NULL | SQL is three-valued: `x IN (…)` over a NULL `x` is NULL, not false, so `p OR (NULL IN …)` can be NULL, and `NOT NULL` is NULL, which a WHERE drops. `heldAtRest()` negates the visibility predicate, and without a guard a failed upload with no verdict would drop out of the reviewers' queue that way | Make a predicate you negate two-valued: guard the nullable column (`and(isNotNull(col), inArray(col, …))`) or `coalesce(…, false)` it, as `passedScreening` in `lib/documents/visibility.ts` does. `visibility.integration.spec.ts` holds the SQL to the in-memory rule over rows with NULLs; a mocked handle cannot see this |
| `toISOString is not a function` on a value `tsc` says is a `Date` | A raw ``sql<Date>`max(...)` `` fragment is a compile-time assertion only. Drizzle decodes column values only for direct column references | Coerce at the repository boundary (`new Date(row.x)`, `Number(row.x)`). See [code-conventions.md](code-conventions.md) |
| A composite foreign key silently permits a bad row | Composite FKs are MATCH SIMPLE, so the check is skipped when **any** column of the key is NULL | Add a CHECK asserting the columns are populated together. `documents_folder_requires_project` is the worked example (widened to `… OR scope = 'office_shelf'` by 0102, where `documents_folder_id_organization_id_scope_fkey` took over the checking). MATCH FULL is the reflex fix and is wrong |
| After a document is deleted, a row that pointed at it keeps a `document_version_id` that names no Fassung, although its version key says `ON DELETE SET NULL (document_version_id)` | The row has two keys: `(document_id, project_id)` with `SET NULL (document_id)` and the composite `(document_version_id, document_id)` with `SET NULL (document_version_id)`. The document's own key fires first and nulls `document_id`; from then on the composite key is MATCH SIMPLE-unchecked (the row above), so its SET NULL matches nothing when the Fassung's cascade arrives. Found on `inbound_mail_parts` and `mail_thread_entry_attachments` (0196) | A `BEFORE UPDATE OF document_id` trigger that sets `document_version_id := NULL` when `document_id` becomes NULL (`inbound_mail_parts_document_guard`, `mail_thread_entry_attachments_guard`), and an rls-test-db.sh assertion that deletes the document and reads both columns back |
| Renaming or moving a folder that has subfolders is a 500: `null value in column "path" of relation "project_folders" violates not-null constraint`, and a leaf folder renames fine | `rewriteDescendantPaths` built `substring(path from <n>)` with `n` bound from JavaScript. A bound number reaches Postgres typed `text`, and `substring(text FROM text)` is the REGEX form, which returns NULL when the pattern does not match, so every descendant's new path was NULL. Every unit test used a doubled drizzle handle, so nothing ever ran the statement. It sat in the project folders for months because the failure needs a folder with children | Compute the offset in SQL: `char_length(${oldPath}::text) + 1`. Cast any bound number a Postgres function overloads on (`${n}::int`). `shelf-folders.integration.spec.ts` renames a subtree against a real database; a mocked-`getDb` spec cannot see this class of error |
| A migration spec or `task db:test:rls` run fails at `initdb` with `could not access directory … Permission denied` although the directory is `chmod 777` | `initdb` and `pg_ctl` refuse root, so the scripts drop to the `postgres` user, which needs EXECUTE on every parent directory. The session scratchpad under `/tmp/claude-<uid>/…` sits below a `0700` directory, and in the cloud session where this was hit `chmod 711` on the parents did not make it reachable (`namei -m` kept showing `drwx------`) | Put the cluster in a directory the `postgres` user can reach (`mkdir /tmp/gridpg && chown postgres /tmp/gridpg`), not under the session scratchpad. `scripts/rls-test-db.sh` already uses `mktemp -d`, which is world-reachable |
| `task db:test:rls` fails in a suite you did not touch, and a rerun fails in a different one or passes: `expected { n: 1 } to match object { n: +0 }` on a count, or `Hook timed out in 10000ms` in a `beforeAll` | The script runs every integration file against ONE database, in parallel workers. A count of a shared or platform table sees the rows another file writes and deletes a moment later (`feedback/repository.integration.spec.ts` holds a `platform_lessons` row), and a 10 s hook runs out when other gates load the machine at the same time | Assert on rows the spec itself made (their ids), never on a table's total. Run `db:test:rls` on its own, not beside `be:verify` or `fe:test:ci`, before you read a hook timeout as a real failure. The lesson-table check in `tenant-isolation.integration.spec.ts` now asserts that the platform read works, not that it counts 0. A hook now has the tests' 50 s (`hookTimeout` in `apps/workspace/vitest.config.ts`), after `inbound-mail/sweep.integration.spec.ts` ran out of 10 s in CI's Workspace job on an unchanged module |
| CI's Website job fails in `playwright install-deps chromium` with `Temporary failure resolving 'security.ubuntu.com'` (and `archive.ubuntu.com`, `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`), on a branch that does not touch the website | The runner could not resolve the Ubuntu mirrors (2026-11-11, three runs within an hour). The image already carries every library Chromium links against; apt was fetching nine optional font packages, and `install-deps` is all or nothing. The job runs for any branch that edits `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | The step is no longer fatal (it warns); `task website:verify` drives Chromium and fails by itself if a library is really missing. If the warning shows on every run, the runner's DNS for the mirrors is the thing to fix (`vars.CI_RUNNER`) |
| A new organization's uploads fail with „Object storage is unavailable“ (502) while every older organization uploads fine, and nothing reached the logs or PostHog | Per-organization buckets (`seaweedfsPerOrgBuckets`): each bucket is a SeaweedFS collection that grows up to 7 volumes, and `seaweedfsVolumeMaxCount` (64 by default) ran out after about nine buckets. The 502 was an `UpstreamError` that `errorResponse` answered without logging, so `onRequestError` never saw it | `kubectl -n grid exec seaweedfs-0 -- wget -qO- http://localhost:9333/dir/status`: `Free` below 7 confirms it. Raise `seaweedfsVolumeMaxCount` (prod: 512); `SEAWEED_PER_ORG_BUCKETS=false` on `frontend` and `bff-jobs` is the no-deploy stopgap (new objects go to the shared bucket, nothing moves). Deleting an organization frees nothing: no code path calls `DeleteBucket`. A 502 now logs `[upstream] …` and goes to PostHog as `source: bff-upstream-error` |
| Every search and every ingest fails with `embedding mismatch: Qdrant collection was written with '...', this process embeds with '...'` on a dev stack that worked last week | The process embeds with another model than the one the Qdrant collection's vectors came from: the code default for `EMBED_MODEL` moved from the NVIDIA embedder to `openai/text-embedding-3-large` on OpenRouter (2026-09), or `deploy/.env` changed. The collection records its embedder in its metadata when it is created (`schema.ensure_collection`), and `QdrantStore.check_identity` refuses reads and writes from any other, rather than mixing two vector spaces | Production sets `EMBED_MODEL` explicitly and is unaffected. For a dev stack: pin the model and base URL the collection names in `deploy/.env`, or drop the dev Qdrant volume and let the OIB sync re-ingest. Never edit the collection metadata to make the error go away: the vectors stay from the other space |
| A WebSocket authorized for one conversation starts, answers, cancels or reattaches to a turn in another; or a new chat frame gets back `error_message` with `conversation_mismatch` | The BFF authorizes only the upgrade's `conversationId` query param. Every `user_message` / `user_interaction` frame carries its OWN `conversation_id`, the runtime copies it into `_conversation_id`, and the registry, the running task, the HITL future and the checkpoint all key on it. The runtime's reattach reads a third copy, the snake_case `conversation_id` query param, which the scope route never sees. Checking the upgrade secures none of these | Closed in `websocket_reconnect.py`: the socket is bound to the envelope's signed `conversationId` at the handshake, `_admit_conversation` refuses any frame naming another, and the reattach follows only the signed id. `test_a_frame_for_another_conversation_runs_nothing` pins it. A `conversation_mismatch` from a real client means it reused a socket across conversations: open a new one. Any NEW frame type that carries a conversation id goes through `_admit_conversation` before it touches state |
| Cached project context comes back belonging to another tenant | `getCached` returns before the loader runs, so a key without the organization serves whatever the first caller populated, never entering the tenant scope | Put the organization in the cache key. `lib/project-profile/prompt-view.ts` is the pattern. Closed structurally on the BFF: `grid/require-tenant-cache-key` (`apps/workspace/eslint-rules/`) fails lint on a `getCached`/`setCached` key with no organization segment, and a key that really is one per deployment goes in `eslint-rules/global-cache-keys.mjs` **with its reason**. The Python tier (`common/cache.py`) has no equivalent yet |
| Platform → Models: the model dial takes, the thinking level does not; the backend log shows `Reasoning effort active` only for `clarifier`, `memory_reflection`, `follow_ups`, `deep_research_router`, never for `shallow_research` or `deep_research` | The effort was applied inside `apply_model_override` only, and every user-facing agent resolves its LLMs through `LLMProvider.with_model_overrides`, which swaps the model and nothing else. Latent since the dial shipped; it surfaced when the YAML floor for `research_llm` moved from `low` to `medium` (43d7cabd) and the dial could no longer bring it back. Every test was green because none exercised the provider seam | `LLMProvider.with_reasoning_efforts(get_reasoning_efforts())` sits in each agent's chain and in the job worker, pinned by `TestProviderWithReasoningEfforts` and `test_active_provider.py`. For a NEW per-group dial, the provider seam is the one a chat turn takes; `apply_model_override` reaches only directly held LLMs. Testing a save: the model needs a new conversation (the map rides the WS upgrade header), the effort shows within its 60 s cache |
| A tenant-scoped query returns rows it should not | The `WHERE organization_id` was lost, widened by a join, or written as a raw fragment | Row-level security is the backstop, not the plan. Check the table joined the boundary via `secure_table` |
| A deep-research job fails one second after submit ("The job failed due to an internal error") and the run block shows nothing after „Planen" | The worker injected the project context **raw** into its request headers, and Starlette `Headers` encode latin-1 only — a German „quote or em-dash in the project profile throws `UnicodeEncodeError` before the agent emits a single event | Free text never goes raw into a header: base64url-encode it (`_b64url_encode_text` in `agent_host/jobs/runner.py`), which is what the read side (`project_context._read_encoded_header`) already expects. `test_runner_safety.py::TestWorkerHeaderTextEncoding` pins it |
| A raw `db.execute(sql\`…\`)` query returns nothing, every mocked test passes, and in production a semantic or deduplication gate never matches | The code read `result.rows`. drizzle over postgres-js resolves `execute()` to a RowList, which is an **array**; `{ rows }` is the node-postgres shape the docs show, and `?? []` turned the `undefined` into "no rows" | Read through `executeRows()` from `lib/db/execute-rows.ts`, which throws on the object shape so a wrong stub fails instead of passing. Stub `execute` with an array. The memory service, platform lessons and feedback all had it |
| A jsonb column holds a JSON **string** (`"{\"id\":\"a0\"}"`) where you wrote an object, `payload->>'id'` is NULL, or a `payload @> …` filter matches nothing, with no error; plain postgres-js and drizzle `sql` both do it | `${JSON.stringify(x)}::jsonb` types the parameter as JSON, so postgres-js serializes the already-serialized string a second time. Only a real database sees it: a mock returns what it was given | Write `${JSON.stringify(x)}::text::jsonb`, which sends text and lets Postgres cast it. `findOpenJobId` and `saveJobProgress` in `lib/jobs-queue/repository.ts` do; `repository.integration.spec.ts` reads a payload back as an object |
| `PostgresError: cannot cast type record to text[]` from a drizzle `sql` fragment with `${array}::text[]` (or `= ANY(${array})`) | drizzle's `sql` spreads a JS array into one parameter per element, `($2, $3, $4)`, which Postgres reads as a row, not an array. A mock never sees it | Send one parameter: `ANY(string_to_array(${array.join(',')}, ','))` for plain tokens (as `scrubbedPayload` in `lib/jobs-queue/repository.ts`), or `${JSON.stringify(array)}::text::jsonb` and `jsonb_array_elements_text`. `repository.integration.spec.ts` holds the dead-row scrub to it |
| A folder upload creates a second „Gebäude" beside the one that is already there | macOS stores filenames **decomposed** (NFD), so a folder dragged off a Mac is `Geba` + `U+0308` + `ude` while the same name typed into Piloti is `Geb` + `U+00E4` + `ude`. They render identically and are different strings, so an exact match finds nothing and creates the near-duplicate | Compare folder names through `folderMatchKey` (`lib/projects/folders.ts`), which normalizes Unicode form, case and whitespace. FILENAMES have their own pair of keys in `lib/documents/name-match.ts` — `documentNameKey` (Unicode form only, because the identity it stands for is case-sensitive in Postgres) and `documentAliasKey` (looser, and may only warn). It is deliberately looser than `uniq_project_folders_parent_name`, which is exact because it exists to stop a race, not to decide what a person may name a folder |
| A folder upload into a project answers 403 „You can read this folder but not change it" for its second level (`Neu/Unterordner`), though the reader writes everywhere, and only in a project with a folder that has its own list or one in the Papierkorb | The upload reads the reader's folder access once, before it creates anything, and `computeFolderAccess` answers `none` for an id its tree does not hold: every folder the upload had just created. A project without such a folder took the open answer, which never asked, so it passed | Closed in `ensureShelfFolderPaths`: a folder the walk inserted itself (`createdHere`) inherits the parent it was asked about, so creating inside it is not asked again. A raced winner is somebody else's folder and is still asked. `folder-service.ensure.spec.ts` holds it |
| `edit_file` on a draft fails with `Error: String not found in file: 'Gebäudeklasse'`, and the string it prints is character for character the one in the file | The two are different bytes. A model typing `ä` decomposed (`a` + U+0308) against a file written composed (U+00E4) hands the exact-string replacement two strings that render identically and do not match, so the model reads the error, concludes it copied wrongly and retries the same bytes. Every German document the working directory holds is exposed to this: `Gebäudeklasse`, `Fläche`, `Überschrift`, `Straße` | Nothing to fix in the model. The working directory NFC-normalises stored content and both `old_string` and `new_string` at the backend seam (`tools/documents/draft_store.py`), which is the same cure `documentNameKey` already applies to filenames. If you add a second consumer of that backend, or a verb of your own, normalise there too — a comparison against stored text that skips it is this bug again |
| A folder re-upload matches its folders and then reports every file inside them as **new** | The folder comparison was normalized and the filename comparison was raw `===`. So a re-synced Einreichung off a Mac found its folders, missed all of its documents, and re-uploaded the whole corpus — and the server, comparing raw too, put a second row beside each one under a name nobody can tell apart from the first | Compare through `documentNameKey`, on both tiers. `uploadDocument` normalizes at admission so new rows are in one form, and `findLiveDocumentByFilename` probes BOTH forms because rows older than that line are whatever arrived |
| Publishing a document version fails with `duplicate key value violates unique constraint "uniq_document_versions_published_per_document"` | The obvious implementation supersedes the previous published version AFTER swapping the new one in, and a PARTIAL unique index makes the instant in between unrepresentable. Doing it the other way round is worse: a supersede that then loses the compare-and-swap race leaves the document with NO published version | Both statements plus the pointer move go in one transaction (`promoteVersionToPublished`, `lib/documents/version-repository.ts`), and the transition table marks such a row `promotes: true` rather than listing them as effects — an effect that ran after the state change would have to leave the database in a state it refuses to hold |
| A `documents` service spec dies with `APP_DATABASE_URL is not defined` after touching nothing near the database | The three upload shelves record a version through `recordUploadedVersion`, which reaches `@/lib/documents/version-repository` and therefore `getDb()`. A suite that mocks `./repository` does not mock the version one | Mock `version-repository` too (and `findDocumentInOrg`, which `recordUploadedVersion` reads the row back with). `src/lib/documents/service.spec.ts` carries the factory to copy. The same error from an upload spec that mocks `@/lib/storage/service` but not `@/lib/storage/upload-limit`: `assertFileSizeAllowed` reads the organization's per-file limit from its settings row. Stub it the way the quota is stubbed there |
| A spec that returns or files a project document dies with `APP_DATABASE_URL is not defined`, or a route spec answers 500 where it expected 200 | Every read of a project document asks the folder-access decision point (`lib/authz/folder-access.ts`, ADR-0087, ADR-0088), which reads `project_folders` through `getDb()` and, for a project with a folder of its own, the folder roles from WorkOS (`lib/authz/folder-roles.ts`, ADR-0097); a spec that stubs `@/lib/db` for its own queries cannot answer it. `getAccessibleDocument`, the listings, the upload and generated-document filing all go through it | `vi.mock('@/lib/authz/folder-access', async () => (await import('@/test-utils/folder-access')).openFolderAccessModule())`: an open project. Override one function with `vi.mocked(…)` to test a restriction. A write also asks `requireFolderWrite`; the open module allows it, so a spec that expects a 403 from the project permission makes the mock delegate to its mocked `requireProjectAccess`, as the real one does (`documents/service.spec.ts`). The pure rule (`folder-access-rule.ts`) is spread into the mock and stays real. A run, a task, a card's profile patch or a filing that names its conversation also asks whether that conversation recorded a restricted folder (`lib/conversations/restricted-egress.ts` → `recordedRestrictedFolders`), and sharing goes through `assertMayWidenConversation` / `widenConversationAudience`: mock `@/lib/conversations/restricted-use` (`sharing/service.spec.ts` and `collaboration/boundaries.spec.ts` are the pattern) |
| A spec that reads or writes a CONVERSATION through the real `@/lib/sharing/access` (a route spec, a service spec) starts dying with `APP_DATABASE_URL is not defined`, or with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'includes')` at `clearanceOf`, although it never touched folders | `resolveResourceAccess` now asks whether the caller may still read what the conversation drew on (`readersAmong` → `peopleWhoMayRead`, ADR-0088): one read of `conversation_restricted_folders` through `getDb()`, and `clearanceOf(session)` of the caller, whose fixture lacked `permissions` | Mock the answer: `vi.mock('@/lib/conversations/restricted-use', async (importOriginal) => ({ ...(await importOriginal<…>()), peopleWhoMayRead: vi.fn(async (_o, _c, ids) => new Set(ids)), lockedConversationIds: vi.fn(async () => new Set()) }))`, and give the session `role`, `roles` and `permissions: []` (`conversations/service.spec.ts` is the pattern). A test that wants the locked state sets `peopleWhoMayRead` to leave the caller out; `allowLocked` callers (the roster, leaving, deleting one's own) are the only ones that pass it |
| CI's frontend shard fails `server-component-db-access.spec.ts` › `no NEW route handler opens the database` (`expected [ …(12) ] to deeply equal [ …(11) ]`) after a route change whose own specs passed | A file under `src/app` imports a value from `@/lib/db` or `@/lib/db/*`, often just a constant from `@/lib/db/schema`. The spec lives under `src/lib/db`, so a targeted run of the area you changed never reaches it | `bun run lint` and the editor now report it (`typescript/no-restricted-imports` in `.oxlintrc.json`). Re-export the constant from the service and import it there; mock it in the route spec's service mock. The legacy offenders are `eslint-rules/route-db-access-allowlist.mjs`, which only shrinks, mirrored in `.oxlintrc.json` (`lint-config.spec.mjs` fails when the two differ) |
| A value typed as an ISO string arrives at a client component as a `Date` | The RSC boundary does not `JSON.stringify` props — it serializes and REBUILDS a `Date`. An API route that returned the same rows was stringified by the response, so the two readers of one query disagreed | Project explicitly at the boundary and have both readers go through it (`lib/documents/list-projection.ts`). The failure surfaces later as `toISOString is not a function`, on whichever surface touches the field first |
| A vitest worker is SIGKILLed on a hook spec that passed yesterday, with no failure printed and no stack | An infinite render loop, which under `act()` never yields and so never reports. The shape to look for: an effect that takes a prop-derived object into state when the comparison is `>=` rather than `>`. A caller that rebuilds the object each render (the chat store rebuilds `message`) then hands over an EQUAL-but-new value, the effect sets state, the render repeats. `useRunLedger`'s `notOlder` had exactly this: equal `updatedAt` is the same frame, and taking it swapped in a new object for identical facts | Compare strictly, and assert identity: rerender with a shallow copy of the same value and expect `toBe` the first object (`use-run-ledger.spec.ts`). The loop is invisible in the product — React schedules it asynchronously and it merely burns CPU — so the test is the only thing that says it |
| Server-rendered content is in the HTML and invisible until the bundle boots | A `motion` component writes its `initial` prop into the SERVER's markup — `initial={{opacity: 0}}` really does render `style="opacity:0"` — so an entrance animation on server-rendered content hides it until hydration releases it. In a browser it completes in 240ms and looks fine; on a slow connection it is a blank page over data that already arrived | `initial={false}` unless something actually navigated (`levelTransition` in `file-browser-pane.tsx`), and assert it: render the component with `renderToStaticMarkup` and match for `opacity:\s*0`. That spec is the only thing that catches it |
| A citation opens its document at page 1 although the chip says „S. 18" | One document carries TWO loci with the same `[N]`: the retrieval payload states the page, the answer's written `## Quellen` list states the same number and names no page. Whichever the binding kept decided the open, and it kept the page-less one | The rule is `openAt` / `referencesByNumber` in `features/chat/lib/citations`: a locus that names a page beats one that does not, at both the `[N]` binding and the open. If you add a third producer of loci, it inherits this — do not resolve a page anywhere else |
| A cited project file offers no way to open it and says nothing about why | It resolved to a real document whose content type has no in-app viewer (`.dwg`, `.ifc`; before ADR-0070 also `.docx`, `.xlsx`), and the target degraded to `info`, which is also what an UNRESOLVABLE citation degrades to. The two are different answers and the reader got the wrong one | `resolveCitationTarget` returns `download` for that case. An office file (`isOfficeRenditionSource`) is a `document` now and opens on its PDF rendition; `SourcePreview` falls back to the download when `/preview` answers 415 or 502. Anything rendering a `CitationTarget` must handle all five kinds; the `info` branch means "nothing is there", never "we cannot draw it" |
| A long Deep-Research-Bericht cannot be downloaded as a PDF, and the footer says „Bad Request" | Two things at once: the render cap counted CHARACTERS, which prices prose like a table and refused a report the renderer lays out in under three seconds; and `useDownloadPdfRoute` surfaced `response.statusText` as the user-facing error | The cap is `markdownRenderCost` (characters + 64 per table cell) against `MAX_MARKDOWN_PDF_CHARS`, and the route answers `413 REPORT_TOO_LONG`. Measure before changing either — the numbers and the method are in `markdown-pdf.ts`'s header, and the shape ratio is ~10x at equal size |
| The viewer opens at the right page and marks nothing; the Fundstelle rail is empty; „Zitat kopieren" gives a bibliography line | The retrieved passage never reached the client. `SourceEntry.chunk_text` was captured, deduplicated and merged on the backend and then dropped in `source_entry_to_wire` — so `locus.snippet` was always undefined and every surface built on it was inert. Nothing failed, because a missing snippet is a supported outcome everywhere it is read | The wire carries `snippet` now, and `test_wire_sources_fixture_matches_the_serializer` asserts the two key SETS are equal. It was `sampled.items() <= live.items()`, a subset — which pins that a field still means what it did and says nothing about a field that stopped being sent. That is what let this, `document_id` and `score` all go missing at once |
| A citation chip lists more pages than the answer used, and the copied citation has no page at all | `citedPages` did not filter on `isCited`, and the `## Trace-Lanes` fan-out contributes a locus per RETRIEVED page. `refPage` then names a page only when there is exactly one, so the same document printed a wrong list and a missing number from one call | `citedPages` is the answer's claim, `readPages` the Herleitung's. They are two different claims and the model exists to keep them apart |
| A cited `.md`, BMP or TIFF says „lässt sich in Piloti nicht anzeigen" for a format the product renders | A third private copy of the previewable content-type list, drifted. `file-preview-pane.tsx` carries a written record of the same drift on the same two types, from the time before | One list: `lib/documents/preview-types.ts`, imported by the service, the pane and the citation resolver, pinned by `preview-types.spec.ts`. It is a pure module with no `server-only` import for exactly this reason |
| An allow-listed outbound fetch reaches a host that is not on the list | The check ran on the URL the caller passed, and the client follows redirects. The allow-list bounded the REQUEST and not the FETCH — one `Location` header later the response body comes from anywhere | Validate every hop: an `event_hooks={"request": [...]}` on the `httpx.AsyncClient` cannot be forgotten by a caller, which a check at each call site can. `packages/ris-adapter/src/client.py` and its two redirect specs are the worked example. A hop on the list can still resolve into the cluster (DNS rebinding, a publisher's own misconfiguration): `packages/public-sources/src/egress.py` also checks the address after DNS and dials the one it checked, `test_egress.py` holds each case |
| A route's comment names a rate limit that does not exist | `resolveLimitRule` defaults MUTATIONS only; reads are left to the edge. „Declared by omission" is true for a mutation and false for a read, and a reviewer greps the comment rather than the resolver | A read that makes a third-party network call earns its own rule in `lib/limits/rules.js` (`RIS_DOCUMENT_LIMIT` is the shape). `BIM_QUERY_LIMIT`/`BIM_EXPORT_LIMIT` are the precedent |
| „Antwort unterbrochen — bitte erneut senden" appears while the agent is plainly still working | The WebSocket inactivity watchdog read silence as death. It cannot be: a single backend LLM call is allowed `request_timeout: 600` in `configs/config_oib_openrouter.yml`, and a deep-research turn is silent on the socket by construction because its progress travels on SSE | Silence is judged by the server's own beat, and only while a turn runs: the socket (`adapters/api/turn-socket.ts`) drops and reopens after three missed `heartbeat{every_ms}` and re-attaches the turn, so a slow turn is never accused. A turn is ended only when the stream no longer holds it (`rejected{turn_not_found}`) or the socket gives up, and then the server is asked for the finished answer before the banner (`_awaitServerAnswer`) |
| „Denkt nach… Ns" counts up forever: the turn has `RUN_STARTED` and heartbeats, and nothing else ever arrives | The heartbeat is an independent task, so it proves the SOCKET's turn task is alive, not that the turn is getting anywhere. Five ways a turn used to live on with no terminal: queued at the runtime's per-replica semaphore (default 8, held for the whole run, a HITL wait of up to 30 min included); a model call or graph that never returned (there was no deadline); a `BaseExceptionGroup` that no `except` named; a Stop whose workflow `finally` hung; and a resend of the same question on another replica running the turn twice | Closed in `agent_host/chat_socket.py`, `workflow_stream.py` and `plugin.py`: the runtime's semaphore is off for the chat (`max_concurrency=0`, ADR-0040 admission refuses at once instead); `CHAT_TURN_DEADLINE_SECONDS` on the turn's own clock, paused while a person is asked; a `finally` that sends a `RUN_ERROR` if no terminal went out; the Stop's teardown wait is bounded; the turn id is claimed on the bus. `test_chat_socket.py` pins each. A new wait inside a turn needs no new timeout of its own; a new wait on a PERSON pauses `RunningTurn.deadline` as `ask` does |
| „Denkt nach… Ns" counts up forever after a question, nothing arrives, the console shows `[turn-socket] not a v2 event, dropped: error_message`, and a reload re-attaches and waits again | The agent tier is not speaking chat wire v2: the dev deploy had rolled it back to an image with the runtime's stock socket, which accepts the upgrade, ignores `?v=2`, never closes with 4426 and answers `user_message` in its own dialect. The version gate only worked one way (4426 refuses an old page), the client dropped the unreadable reply, and its watchdog watched nothing before `RUN_STARTED` | Closed on both sides. The server's first frame is `hello`, and the page sends nothing until it arrives: no hello in 5 s, or anything else first, fails the attempt and ends `connection.server_incompatible`. Every question has 15 s to be answered before its socket is reopened, and a second miss ends it with an error card; an unreadable frame after the hello is a reload notice, never dropped ([`websocket-protocol.md`](../api/websocket-protocol.md#the-client)). If you see „Piloti ist gerade nicht erreichbar" on dev, check which image the agent runs: `/health` answers `sha` |
| A citation to a file uploaded during THIS conversation says there is nothing to open | The chip resolves through two module-level indexes (`SourcePreview`'s and `use-surfaced-documents`'), both held for the page lifetime and populated on first use. An upload, a rename or a delete never invalidated them, so the resolver answered from a listing taken before the file existed | Fire `notifyDocumentsChanged()` (`lib/documents/document-changes.ts`) from anything that changes the document set. TWO halves: the caches subscribe at module scope to be dropped, and a mounted surface puts `useDocumentsGeneration()` in its load effect's deps — clearing a module `Map` re-renders nothing, so without the second half a rename or delete (which always happens while the surface showing that document is mounted) does not reach the screen until a remount. Anything new that caches a document listing subscribes there too — the alternative is a third cache that goes stale in its own way |
| A copied citation names a page where an architect expects a Punkt | The chunker measures the Punkt, the wire carries it and `CitationLocus` holds it — and every export read `page` only. „OIB-Richtlinie 2, S. 12" identifies a printing; „Pkt. 3.5.2" identifies the requirement | CSL `section` carries it (`shared/citation-style.ts`), printed before the page in the style's `bareLocus` form. The same nesting bug hid it in the Fundstelle line: a Punkt was only ever read INSIDE `page != null`, so a locus with a Punkt and no page read „Gesamtes Dokument" |
| A field added to the citation wire reaches a live tab and is gone after a reload | Two independent normalizers stand between the wire and storage, and both ENUMERATE the fields they keep: `agent-answer-metadata.ts` (every row the Python side persists — deep research, and any answer whose socket was gone mid-turn) and `prune-message-for-storage.ts` (the localStorage copy). A field listed in neither is dropped silently, on exactly the turns a reader comes back to | Add it to both, with its own bound. Sizing matters as much as presence: the passage matchers anchor on BOTH ends of a snippet, so a snippet clipped to a `content`-sized 300 chars downgrades „the sentence is marked" to „the page is open" with nothing on screen saying why |
| A Herleitung card says „abgerufen, nicht zitiert" and names no pages at all | `citedPages` is the answer's claim and returns nothing for a document with no cited locus — which is every document that card type stands for. The chip under the answer and the card in the Herleitung print one line of markup and make two different claims | `documentPages` is what a SURFACE prints: precise when a binding exists, honest when it does not (the same widening `answerDocuments` already does for the row). `citedPages`/`readPages` stay for the two specific claims |
| A dialog that opens a large document freezes the tab for half a second, and the tests are all green | The passage matcher folds EVERY character through `normalize('NFKD')` plus two Unicode regexes, and builds two arrays of one object per character. Correct and free on a PDF page; 2M characters of a consolidated law is 670 ms and ~317 MB of heap, on the main thread, inside a `useMemo` | Latin-1 folds through a table built by the slow path itself (so it cannot drift), and the whole-text path keeps parallel `string[]`/`number[]` instead of objects. Measure the CLIENT before raising a server-side size cap — the first version of `MAX_DOCUMENT_TEXT_CHARS` was justified with a browser LAYOUT measurement, which is not the cost that grows |
| A recovered answer has „bitte erneut senden" printed under it | `_recoverInterruptedAssistantMessage` returned a boolean, and `false` meant three different things: nothing on the server, a concurrent recovery already appended it, and a newer turn is streaming. Recovery runs on mount, on reconnect and from the streaming watchdog, and any two can be in flight at once | `RecoveryOutcome` — only `nothing` earns the banner. A predicate whose `false` covers "no" and "someone else already did it" will be read as the first by whoever calls it |
| Two different sources render as one chip, and its page opens the wrong document | The canonical OIB key answers "does this name MENTION OIB-Richtlinie 6", which is right for a `legal_basis` card (whose `law` field is the law) and wrong for a file: „OIB-Richtlinie 6 Kommentar.pdf" is a commentary ABOUT it. The shelf rule cannot catch it — a source known only from the answer's written list carries no shelf at all | The file side goes through `oibCorpusKey`'s residue test: strip the number and every word the key already models, and what is left must be nothing. Any rule of the form "the name contains X" is a merge waiting to happen; ask whether the name IS the thing |
| A task run (Änderungen anfordern on a long Piloti document, say) is `failed` the moment it is created, and its error is a 422 validation dump naming `input` and `string_too_long` | The BFF composed a prompt longer than the backend's `SkillSubmitPayload.input` ceiling. The revision quote had its own 60,000-character limit above the backend's 48,000, so every document between the two failed at submission | Budget against `AGENT_RUN_INPUT_MAX_CHARS` (`lib/jobs/types.ts`), never a number of your own: it mirrors `SUBMIT_INPUT_MAX_CHARS` in `routes/skills.py`, `test_skill_submit.py` holds the two equal, and `submitAgentRun` refuses an over-long prompt in a sentence before sending it. Do not cut a document the run will file back: its answer replaces the whole version |
| A client-side constant mirrors a server-side one, and there is no way to keep them in step | The client was predicting the server — a copy of `DEFAULT_MAX_RUN_SECONDS` used to decide when a silent turn was dead, so the two could drift and the reader paid either way (a healthy turn accused, or a dead one waited out for forty minutes) | Put the number on the wire and make the client's own constant a TOLERANCE over it: the `heartbeat` event carries `every_ms`, the socket holds the tolerance (`DEAD_AFTER_BEATS`). A frame the receiver can observe beats a constant it has to predict, and the fallback for a peer that does not send it is per-turn rather than global |
| `userEvent.click()` on a hover-revealed overlay action does nothing — the query finds the button, `fireEvent.click()` on the same node works | userEvent's multi-step pointer sequence (pointerdown → focus → mouseup → click) re-renders the motion row mid-gesture: focusing the button flips the hover/focus state the overlay's visibility depends on, the node detaches, and the click lands on a node React no longer listens to | Reveal with `user.hover`, then click with `fireEvent.click` and say why in a comment. `SessionsPanel.spec.tsx` (the stuck-research purge tests) is the worked example |
| An answer about a whole Richtlinie is fluent, every citation resolves, and one part of it was never opened (OIB-RL 2 answered from 2, 2.1 and 2.2, with no 2.3) | The prompt said „OIB-Richtlinien 1–6", which is a RANGE and names no members, and the Basiswissen shelf is folded to a count. Retrieval can recover filenames; it cannot recover which parts EXIST — a search that never returns 2.3 looks exactly like a Richtlinie without one, so nothing structural could notice | The folded shelf carries one line per family with the members the corpus actually holds (`norm_registry.oib_families`, derived from indexed filenames and set BEFORE the inventory cap, which drops base rows first). `<research_rules>` requires every listed member to be opened; `status:coverage:<family>` counts listed against opened so the miss rate is countable. Anything else that folds a shelf to a shape inherits this: fold the list, never the fact that a member exists |
| A `ContextVar` set in one LangGraph node reads back as `None` in the next one, and its unit tests are all green — e.g. every Trace-Lanes hit ships with no `round` and the Herleitung hangs both fetches on the first checkpoint | LangGraph runs each node in a task created with `copy_context()`. A node mutates its OWN copy: the value never reaches the next node, and never reaches a tool, because `ToolNode` runs in the tools node's context. Setting it in the agent node is writing to a scratch copy that is discarded at the node boundary | Set it in the node where the reader actually runs, in a scope that resets — `retrieval_round_scope` around `ToolNode.ainvoke` in `piloti/agent.py::_tools_node`. Parallel tool calls DO inherit it, because sibling tasks copy the context when they are created. A test that sets the var and calls the reader passes on the broken code; the only test that can fail goes through the compiled graph and reads the var inside a tool (`packages/agent/tests/agents/piloti/test_retrieval_rounds_spine.py`) |
| A trace tag a tool adds with `add_trace_tag` shows on the tool's own spans but not on the turn's root observation, so a rule or filter that reads the root's tags (`chat-turn-judges`'s `none of`) misses the turn | `add_trace_tag` sets a new dict in a `ContextVar` (copy-on-write), and the span processor stamps each span from the context its end event fires in. The tool runs in a node's copied context; the root span ends in the turn's own, which never saw the write | Also call `turn_outcome.record_turn_tag(tag)`: the turn-outcome box is a dict bound in the socket's context and mutated in place, so a write from any copy reaches the root (`licensed_text.mark_licensed_turn` is the worked example; `test_licensed_seam.py::test_the_licensed_tag_reaches_the_turns_root_observation_from_inside_a_tool`) |
| Langfuse shows "Waiting for first trace" / 0 traces with all pods Ready | The ClickHouse PVC is full — and the bulk is usually the server's own system logs (`trace_log`, `text_log`), which the stock image ships with no TTL, not trace data. The worker logs `Cannot reserve ... not enough space` and drops every batch | `df` the PVC and list tables by size (queries in `docs/deployment/kubernetes.md` §9b). System logs are TTL-bounded at 14 days by `system-log-ttl.xml` (`deploy/pulumi/src/data/clickhouse.ts`), so a full disk means a pre-TTL deployment (clear the system logs once) or trace data itself outgrowing the PVC (patch the PVC; default 50Gi) |
| A thumbs vote is stored but no `user-feedback` score appears on any Langfuse trace, and the platform view's `langfuseTraceUrl` is null for that turn | The BFF scores only the trace the answer ROW names (`messages.metadata.trace_id`) and never derives one, so a turn from before the agent recorded traces, or one whose answer was never persisted, has none. Otherwise the BFF has no `LANGFUSE_HOST` or keys (scoring is a silent no-op by design, with no Langfuse Cloud default), or `allow-frontend-to-langfuse` is missing and every call times out in the background | Check the row first: `select metadata->>'trace_id' from messages where id = '<messageId>'`. Then the frontend env (`LANGFUSE_HOST`, both keys) and, for the link, `LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_URL` + `LANGFUSE_PROJECT_ID`. A failed write logs `[Langfuse] score upsert failed`; it never fails the vote (ADR-0044, Amendment 3) |
| Every Langfuse login fails with `invalid_scope` right after a deploy, operators included | The edge's OIDC request asks WorkOS for every scope the SecurityPolicy checks, and WorkOS rejects a request for a scope not assigned to the Connect application. Adding `platform:observability:view` to the gate (ADR-0089) without assigning it first locks everyone out | Assign the scope on the Connect application in the WorkOS dashboard BEFORE `npm run up`. And one Envoy rule listing several scopes needs a token holding ALL of them, so each admitted permission gets its own rule (`deploy/pulumi/src/platform/platform-oidc.ts`) |
| A Langfuse trace is filed under environment "default", has no release, or every observation is a plain span | Langfuse reads `langfuse.environment`, `langfuse.release` and `langfuse.observation.type` from the SPAN. `deployment.environment` on the resource, `service.version` on the logs resource and the runtime's `nat.span.kind` are all ignored without a word | `TraceContextProcessor` (`observability/langfuse_trace_attributes.py`) writes all three on every span from `APP_ENV`, `GIT_SHA` and the span kind. If they are missing, check it is in the exporter's pipeline (`trace_context`) and that the pod has `GIT_SHA` baked in |
| Page reads (`ingest-vision`) show a cache rate near 1% although the fixed prompt leads every call and `session_id` / `prompt_cache_key` are sent | A manual `provider.order` disables OpenRouter's sticky routing ("your explicit ordering takes priority"), and the ZDR policy's EU-first preference is exactly such an order, so each call landed on whichever endpoint came first and its cache was cold | Use `provider.only` when caching matters: it filters the endpoints and leaves sticky routing on. ZDR page reads do (`DataPolicy.for_page_reads`: `only` the preferred EU slugs, `allow_fallbacks: false`, no `order`); every other call keeps the order. A "no allowed providers" refusal is then transient (`ingest_failures.no_allowed_provider`) |
| Some `ingest-vision` generations are not under an `ingest-file` trace in Langfuse (12% on 2026-11-02), though every vision pool copies the context and the spans' parent ids are right | A child generation wrote `langfuse.trace.name`, `langfuse.trace.tags` and `langfuse.trace.metadata.feature` like a root does, and Langfuse reads trace-level attributes off ANY span of the trace, so whichever span it ingested last named the trace: often the root (ended last), sometimes a page read | `direct_trace.generation_start_attributes(nested=True)`: a generation under an `observed_span` writes its metadata as `langfuse.observation.metadata.*` and no trace attribute. Held by `test_a_nested_generation_never_claims_its_files_trace`. Re-measure the orphan share after the deploy: a file whose worker died before its root span ended loses the root too, and that share is what remains |
| A tool's trace tags (`feature:ifc`), the usage rollup or the dialect census are missing from Langfuse on one stack and present on another | They used to ride the identity processor, which is installed only with `TRACE_IDENTITY_ATTRIBUTES=true`, so a stack with identity off dropped them silently | They have their own processor now (`trace_contributions` when identity is off). Gate only the user and the tenant on identity; labels, counts and scores never (ADR-0089) |
| No runtime-check scores (`citation-health`, `dialect-repairs`, …) appear on traces, while `user-feedback` does | The agent posts scores itself (`observability/langfuse_scores.py`) and is a no-op without `LANGFUSE_HOST` and both keys on the BACKEND pods, or without `allow-backend-to-langfuse`. A score name not in `SCORE_DEFINITIONS` is refused before it is sent | Check the backend env and the NetworkPolicy, then `task langfuse:provision` for the score configs. A trace with no pinned id (no `workflow_trace_id` in context) gets no scores by design |
| `GET /api/public/traces` answers 404 on our Langfuse, or the purger / scheduler logs `Langfuse GET /api/public/v2/observations answered 404` | Langfuse v4 switches its legacy read endpoints off once it writes `events_only` (`GET /api/public/traces` is `rejectInEventsOnlyMode`), and the v2 observations API exists only in a v4 write mode (`LANGFUSE_MIGRATION_V4_ALLOW_PREVIEW_OPT_IN`). The delete endpoint, `DELETE /api/public/traces`, is not switched off, which is why only the listing breaks | List through `GET /api/public/v2/observations` (what `workers/langfuse-traces.js` does). A 404 from it means the Langfuse is in write mode `legacy` or on v3, and the step fails loudly on purpose rather than reporting an empty store. Do not "fix" it by reading an empty list as done |
| Traces show no cached-token bucket and the input cost does not drop across a turn's iterations | Three separate faults, and the usual mistake is to treat them as one. (a) OpenRouter caches an OpenAI-family prefix automatically and its usage accounting is always on — there is no `usage: {include: true}` to forget, it is deprecated and inert — but a cache lives inside ONE upstream endpoint, and without a sticky key OpenRouter only starts pinning *after* it has seen a hit, which a fleet spread over several endpoints may never produce. (b) On `api_type: responses` the numbers arrive and are then dropped in-process: langchain-openai's Responses path returns a `ChatResult` with NO `llm_output` and a `response_metadata` that omits `usage`, so the provider object carrying `cost`, `cache_discount` and `prompt_tokens_details` never reaches us, and the runtime's span exporter forwards only `llm.token_count.prompt/completion/total`. (c) the per-generation usage was read off the first usage-shaped object found anywhere in the span's `nat.metadata`, and that JSON is the LLM_START payload merged into the LLM_END one — so `chat_inputs`, the whole prompt including earlier AIMessages carrying the usage of the call that produced them, was scanned beside `chat_responses`. Every generation after the first then reported an older answer's numbers: five generations in one turn, byte-identical counts, while the context grew every round | Do not chase the request body: `common/prompt_caching.py` derives a `session_id` + `prompt_cache_key` from the org, model, system prompt and tool set, and `common/llm_factory.py` attaches them to every OpenRouter call. For the counts, read LangChain's normalized `usage_metadata` — `input_token_details.cache_read` is the cache bucket under another name — which `cost_tracking.extract_usage_event` and `observability/langfuse_trace_attributes.extract_provider_usage` now both fall back to. `cost` is on the provider's Responses `usage` too, and `cost_tracking.install_responses_cost_carrier` keeps it for the ledger (next row); a reply without one says `costSource: missing` rather than inventing one. Never add `provider.order` to "help": a manual order DISABLES the sticky routing the key exists to drive. For (c), `extract_provider_usage` now reads the response side only — `chat_responses`, the one `TraceMetadata` key `on_llm_end` fills on both the chat-completions and the Responses path — and returns None when that side carries no usage, which falls back to `llm.token_count.*` instead of answering from the prompt |
| Every answer's details show the same tiny cost ("0,1 Punkte") whatever the answer did, and the organization's budget barely moves | The main answer role (`research_llm`) is on `api_type: responses`. OpenRouter sends `usage.cost` there, but langchain-openai's `_construct_lc_result_from_responses_api` keeps only the token counts, so every answer call was written to `llm_usage_events` at cost 0, which prices to 0 credits. The ~0.1 is the post-answer stages (follow-ups, memory reflection), which run on chat completions | `cost_tracking.install_responses_cost_carrier` copies `cost` and `is_byok` onto `response_metadata["grid_usage_accounting"]`; `TestResponsesApiCost` runs a real Responses `ChatOpenAI`, streamed and not, and fails if a langchain-openai upgrade moves the seam. Rows written before the fix stay at 0; query `cost_source = 'missing'` with nonzero tokens to find them |
| The Herleitung shows the same four documents under two steps, or the model fetches the same files twice | Three causes at once, each on its own layer: the Punkt chunker emits every Richtlinie's cover page and Impressum as `chunking="page"` chunks and they are the only ones still carrying the running title, so a title-shaped query ranked four cover pages first; nothing could ask a document what it CONTAINS, so an overview question guessed a Punkt, was refused and searched again; and the prompt asked for a family-wide fan-out in one round that the turn's research budget could not pay for | Closed on all three: `_NON_EVIDENCE_CHUNKING` in `packages/knowledge-layer/src/search/scope.py` keeps page chunks out of every base-corpus search, `read_passage(document=…)` with no Punkt or page returns the outline (`packages/knowledge-layer/src/read_passage/tool.py`), and a fetch whose signature already ran this turn is withheld uncharged (`fetch_signature` in `packages/agent/src/agent/common/tool_calls.py`, `emit_repeat_fetch` in `packages/agent/src/agent/common/turn_notices.py`, `executed_fetches` on Piloti's state). In a trace: `status:repeat:N` counts what was withheld, and a round whose ledger entry has an empty `new_docs` returned nothing the turn did not already hold |
| A tool returns `(text, payload)` with `response_format="content_and_artifact"` and every reader downstream sees `ToolMessage.artifact is None`, while the text arrives intact | The runtime builds the LangChain tool itself. `langchain_tool_wrapper` (`nat/plugins/langchain/tool_wrapper.py`) calls `NATStructuredTool.from_function(coroutine=fn.acall_invoke, ...)` and passes no `response_format`, so the tool runs on LangChain's default `"content"` and never splits a `(content, artifact)` pair. `acall_invoke` has already converted the return value to the function's declared output type on the way out, so the tuple does not reach the message either. Nothing warns: an absent artifact is a supported outcome everywhere it is read, so the feature looks wired and carries nothing | Do not put structured data on the artifact, and do not fork the wrapper for one tool. Return the text, and carry the records beside it in a per-turn `ContextVar` keyed by the text's hash. `common/grounding_block.py` is the worked example, and ADR-0061 is why |
| The Herleitung draws a „Schritt N · Suche" layer with nothing under it — on a measurement turn, one per model call — and the answer is fine | A round is announced as a retrieval by its TOOL NAME (`_SEARCH_CORPORA` in `common/tool_calls.py`: `ifc_`, `surface_documents`, …) but the layer's fan is drawn from the LANE HITS the tool recorded, and only the evidence tools (knowledge, RIS, web) recorded any. An `ifc_query`, `ifc_measure` or `surface_documents` round therefore entered the ledger with `hits: 0`, byte-identical to a search that found nothing; the frontend's one word for both was `Suche` | Every tool in `_SEARCH_CORPORA` records what it read: the IFC tools file the model with the operation as its locus (`tools/bim/measurement_sources.note_model_read`), `surface_documents` files each surfaced file on its shelf. A ledger round that still has no documents is now a miss and reads `Kein Treffer` (`roundKind` in `ReasoningFlow.tsx`); a turn stored before the ledger keeps `Suche`, because there an empty layer may only be an unstamped hit. Adding a corpus prefix to `_SEARCH_CORPORA` obliges the tool to record lane hits, or its rounds read as misses |

The shape the model was taught was not the shape it was validated against | `catalog._is_discriminator` skips a `type` field only when its annotation has one value. Pinned by `packages/agent/tests/cards/test_envelope_cards.py::TestTheRenderedShapeIsTheValidatedShape`, which writes a card FROM the rendered shape and validates it — the check that every rendered shape should have had |

| A layout-shift entry says an element moved `dy +228, dh +391` (or `dy -409, dh -75`), but nothing moved that way on screen | A shift source's `previousRect` and `currentRect` are the VISIBLE parts of the element, clipped to the viewport. An element that was entirely off screen has an empty previous rect, so rising 1,400 px from below the fold into view reads as appearing at y=228 with 391 px height; one shoved past the scroller's bottom edge reads as moving to y=0 with no height. The dy/dh of such a source is not a distance | Treat a source whose previous or current rect height is 0 as one that ENTERED or LEFT the viewport. `measure-stream-socket.mjs` reports those apart as `shiftsEdge`, and `/dev/stream-replay` flags them `edge`. For how far the reader's text really went, read `reading.firstProseMaxDelta` / `lastLineMaxDelta`, or record the element's rect every frame |
| The streaming harness reports a clean turn (CLS 0.02), and the answer still visibly jumps right after it finishes | The probe stopped at the settle.
