| Vectors read back from Chroma are not the float32 values you wrote (none of 2,500 matched the nearest float32 of the input), and an export from the persist directory differs from one over HTTP in the last bit of about one vector in five (at most 1.2e-7), with every id, text, metadata and digest equal | Chroma 1.5.9's write path and its two read paths (the Rust bindings in-process, JSON from the server) each round differently; what it returns is float32, just not the same float32 on both paths | Compare against what Chroma returns (`collection.get(include=["embeddings"])`), not against the input, and compare vectors with a tolerance; a cosine does not see the difference. The exporter writes exactly what its read path returned (`deploy/migration/tests`) |
| A person with a custom WorkOS role uses the app normally, but their scheduled task, mail import or other background job is refused at once | A pinned session takes its permissions from the catalog's list for the person's role (`sessionFor` in `lib/auth/pinned-session.ts`, `permissionsForOrgRole`); a role slug the catalog does not list holds nothing, and the live session's JWT is not consulted | Give the person a catalog role. The fix is for the pinned session to read the role's permissions from WorkOS ([`workos-provisioning.md`](../deployment/workos-provisioning.md), Custom roles) |
| A project purge fails on a trigger that says a table is deleted "only by the platform role", although the purger runs as the platform role | The table has an FK to the project. PostgreSQL runs an `ON DELETE CASCADE` as the owner of the referencing table, so `current_user` in a `BEFORE DELETE` trigger is the owner, never the caller. 0119 escapes this only by having no foreign keys | Do not decide on `current_user` in a delete guard on a table with FKs: `REVOKE DELETE … FROM app_rw` and let the trigger refuse UPDATE only ([row-level-security.md](../database/row-level-security.md#append-only-tables)) |
| A route that reads a raw body (`request.arrayBuffer()`, a stream) gets fewer bytes than the client sent, with no error; a multipart route answers 413 `PayloadTooLargeError` | `experimental.proxyClientMaxBodySize` (`next.config.ts`, from `requestBodyLimitBytes()` in `shared/config/request-body-limit.ts`) does not reject an oversized body: Next buffers up to the limit, logs a warning and hands the route the partial body ([Next docs](https://nextjs.org/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/proxyClientMaxBodySize), read 2026-10-22). Only `parseFormData` (`lib/api/handler.ts`) turns the truncated multipart stream into a 413. Envoy adds no size cap, so this limit is the only one | Keep the ceiling at or above the largest body any route admits (it follows `BIM_MAX_IFC_BYTES` and `FILE_UPLOAD_MAX_SIZE_MB`). A raw-body route compares the bytes it read with the length it expected and refuses a mismatch (`readBoundedBody` in `lib/api/bounded-body.ts`, which `lib/mail-import/service.ts` reads its archive parts through). The proxy truncates only the paths its matcher (`src/proxy.ts`) covers: a route that takes a raw body from outside the cluster lives under `/api/internal/signed/`, which the matcher excludes, and reads it with `readBoundedBody` / `streamBodyTo`, which refuse a short body as 503 `body-truncated` |
| A Markdown escape is green against a CommonMark parser, yet the knowledge layer still cuts a section at a stranger's `## …` line (a mail body after a form feed, a NEL `\x85`, `\x1c`–`\x1e` or `\u2028`), or still reads `##\x1fTitel` as a heading | The chunker (`text_formats.py`) splits with Python's `str.splitlines()`, which ends a line at `\v`, `\f`, `\x1c`–`\x1e`, `\x85`, `\u2028` and `\u2029` as well as `\r`/`\n`, and its heading pattern's `\s` also matches `\x1c`–`\x1f` and `\x85`. JavaScript's `^` in a `m` regex and its `\s` know fewer, so an escape keyed on them leaves those line starts alone | Escape with `lib/text/markdown-escape.ts`, whose line start and space are the union of both readers' (and whose indent is whitespace that is no line terminator: a `\s*` after a line-start lookbehind runs on over every blank line after it, quadratic on a mail of whitespace-only lines), and prove a writer against `@/test-utils/markdown-chunker` (the chunker's reading in TypeScript) as well as remark. Fold a value meant to be one line with a class that includes `\x1c`–`\x1f` and `\x85`, not `\s` alone (`oneLine` in `lib/mail-import/naming.ts`) |
| A zip attachment with a member over 100 MB uncompressed comes back `stream: OK` from clamd although `AlertExceedsMax yes` is set; the same content gzipped gives `Heuristics.Limits.Exceeded.MaxFileSize` | clamd 1.5.4's unzip trims a member to `MaxFileSize` and scans its first 100 MB without raising the limit alert (`cli_unzip: trimming output size to maxfilesize` in `clamscan --debug`, measured 2026-10-22) | A known engine gap, recorded in ADR-0157 ("Consequences") and for G6. There is no client-side fix: the BFF maps clamd's reply exactly (`lib/malware-scan/`). The person still decides before anything from outside is filed |
| A zod schema with `.catch(…)` on a field still parses, but a producer that writes a wrong value into it (a job payload, a request body) compiles without complaint | `.catch()` widens the schema's INPUT type to `unknown` for that field: `z.input<typeof schema>` (what `enqueueJob` and a typed request builder check against) no longer names the allowed values, so the compiler stops guarding the writer (checked with zod 3.25 on 2026-10-22) | Keep `.catch` off a schema a producer is typed by, and be tolerant in the consumer instead: `officeRenditionPayloadSchema.documentType` is a `z.string()` the job reads through `documentTypeOf`, so a retired token reads as no choice while the producer stays typed `DocumentTypeTag` |
| Langfuse traces of a Piloti turn stop after the first agent round: no later LLM generation, tool or retrieval observation, and PostHog Logs shows `No parent span found for step <uuid>` paired one-for-one with `No subspan found for step <uuid>` from `nat.observability.exporter.span_exporter` | LangChain runs `on_chain_end`/`on_llm_end` in a new task on a COPY of the context (`@shielded`), so the runtime's END never restores the span stack: after a LangGraph node and its conditional-edge router, the ended node's id stays on top and the next node STARTs under it. `SpanExporter` drops that START and its whole subtree. Upstream in nvidia-nat 1.9.0 | `install_step_stack_repair` (`agent/common/nat_step_repair.py`) pops ended ids off the stack before each START; it is installed at startup in the API server and the agent worker. `test_nat_step_stack_repair.py` runs a real LangGraph loop and fails without it. Delete the repair when the runtime keeps the stack without the context |
| A re-typed document keeps its stale permit record, and the ingest worker warns `permit_records: the BFF refused the record for document <uuid> (HTTP 400)` | The delete (`record: null`) carries `model: ""`, since nothing was extracted, and the BFF's body schema required a non-empty `model` on every body. The route specs always sent `"m"` | Fixed in `lib/permits/types.ts`: `model` is required only with a record. A wire contract's spec sends what the real caller sends, empty strings included |
| A turn answers with no retrieval at all, and the agent warns `Research turn has every data source switched off; every retrieval call will be refused` | The composer sent its initial `enabledDataSourceIds: []` before `/v1/data_sources` had loaded (or after it failed), and on the wire `[]` selects NO source, while absent/`null` selects every source the organization allows | `collectSendMetadata` sends `null` while `availableDataSources` is still `null`. Never send a store's not-yet-loaded default as a deliberate choice |
| The ingest worker fails a burst of files with `Download of the original failed: ConnectError` or `RemoteProtocolError` (the template is `deferred_files.py`'s `Download of the %s failed`, so a grep for the full sentence finds nothing); the BFF logs `[db] database unavailable (EHOSTUNREACH)` in the same minute | The object store, or the BFF behind it, was unreachable for a few seconds. A connect was retried, a response cut short was not | `DeferredObjectDownload` connects through `agent/common/connect_retry.py` (`httpx.HTTPTransport(retries=…)`: a connect is repeated, nothing that reached the store is), and `_get_original` repeats a GET the store cut short (`RemoteProtocolError`, `ReadError`). Every other BFF and store call of the worker (page text, images, thumbnail, presence, permit record) goes through the same client |
| An integration spec's seeded document vanishes for `SCREENED_ONLY` (`findWritableThread(..., SCREENED_ONLY)` is null, a follow waits as `document_held`) although its `status` is `completed` and nothing quarantined it | `documents` rows authored by a person (`authored_by` defaults to `'user'`) pass screening only while `screened_hash IS NOT DISTINCT FROM content_hash` (migration 0123, `passedScreening` in `lib/documents/visibility.ts`): setting `content_hash` alone leaves the bytes unscreened. A member reader still sees it if they uploaded it, so a spec reading as the uploader passes and the screened-only path fails | Seed `content_hash` and `screened_hash` together (both null works too), and have a mocked `uploadDocument` that changes `content_hash` set `screened_hash` with it (`mail-thread/follow.integration.spec.ts`) |
| `MailThreadRenderError: … cannot be rendered: heading_mismatch` from a spec that seeded a `mail_thread_entries` row | The renderer re-derives every stored heading from the entry's own fields (sent time in Vienna, sender name, signer or sender domain, trust, source) and refuses a row whose heading says something else | Build the seeded heading with `entryHeadingBase(fields, locale)` from `lib/mail-thread/render.ts` over the same fields you insert |
| A spec that "races" two writes with `Promise.all` stays green after you delete the lock it was written for | Two short transactions started from one Node process rarely interleave; the first usually commits before the second reads, so the race the lock guards never happens in the test | Make the race deterministic: open a second transaction that takes the same lock (`FOR UPDATE`, the same `pg_advisory_xact_lock` key), start the write, then commit a change under the lock and assert the write saw it (`holding()` in `mail-thread/follow.integration.spec.ts`). Revert-check it: delete the lock and watch the spec fail |
| A `sensitivity` operation (`control`, `access`, `secret`) is missing from `search-ranking.spec.ts` results, from the tools-mode listing in `mcp-server.spec.ts`, and from every external principal's `visibleOperations` | `SENSITIVE_SCOPE_ENFORCED_BY_WORKOS` in `lib/agent-api/policy.ts` is `false` until the step-up scope is set up in WorkOS, so `maySeeSensitive` answers no for every outside agent, and never anything but no for the agent. The operation is catalogued and in `exposure.json`, but no door lists it yet, so it does not count against the tools-mode average either | Test its exposure with `maySeeSensitive(principal, sensitivity, true)` (`exposure.config.spec.ts`, the `mail-thread-follow` case), keep its description short anyway (it joins the average the day the flag flips), and leave it out of the search-ranking queries |
| A route that parses a stranger's bytes under `asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.to_thread(...))` answers "timeout" on time, yet the API pod sits at 100% CPU for a minute afterwards | `wait_for` over a thread only stops waiting; the thread runs on. `mail-parser-reply` 1.36's German „Am … schrieb …:“ pattern is cubic in a line that repeats "schrieb" (3,000 characters: 64 s), and its signature pattern starts with `\s*^`, quadratic in a whitespace run (20,000 tabs: 7.5 s); input caps bound neither | Parse in a child process that can be killed: `read_message_within` in `aiq_api/mail/read.py` runs `python -I -m agent_host.mail.read` with an empty environment and kills it at the deadline. `multiprocessing` does not fit inside NAT (`spawn`/`forkserver` re-import the parent's `__main__`, `fork` copies a threaded process). Bound whitespace runs before the splitter (`mail/replies.py`) and keep the seeded mutation loop (`tests/mail/test_hostile.py`) |
| A mail body converted from HTML raises `ValueError: All strings must be XML compatible`, or a charset lookup raises `ValueError: embedded null character` | libxml2 parses past characters XML forbids and lxml refuses to write them; `codecs.lookup` raises `ValueError`, not `LookupError`, for a name with a NUL | Strip XML-forbidden characters before parsing (`_NOT_XML` in `mail/body_text.py`) and catch `ValueError` around `codecs.lookup` |
| A mail thread's name or a match shows in lower case („einreichplan“) | `baseSubject` (`lib/mail-thread/mail-threading.ts`) is the matching key: RFC 5256's base subject, NFC and case-folded | Never show `base_subject`; show the subject as sent, or the thread document's name |
| One domain counts as two senders or two participants (`müller.at` and `xn--mller-kva.at`, `MÜLLER.AT`) | Domains arrive as U-labels, A-labels and in any case or width | Compare domains only through `domainOf` (`mail-threading.ts`), which answers the A-label as the WHATWG URL parser (UTS #46) makes it |
| A decision on a held mail is refused `stale` although nobody else touched it | `ifMatch` (`mailIfMatch`, `lib/inbound-mail/decision.ts`) hashes the mail's and every part's `updated_at`; a background write that bumps one makes every open decision stale | A background write to `inbound_mail_parts` must not touch `updated_at`: the suggest job stores its suggestion without it |
| A mail with two authors shows one sender | postal-mime's `email.from` keeps only the first address of the first From field | Read every From field from the raw header block (`lib/inbound-mail/mime.ts`); the sender check refuses more than one From anyway |
| mailauth says `dkim=neutral` for a mail you expected to fail, and its own `Authentication-Results` says `spf=temperror` | In its default mode mailauth reports a body-hash mismatch as `neutral (body hash did not verify)` and an expired `x=` as `neutral (expired)`; called without the connecting IP (Email Routing gives none) its SPF is always `temperror` | `sender-auth.ts` names them `dkim_fail` and `dkim_expired`, never reads SPF, and stores our own line from DKIM and DMARC only; a CHECK refuses a stored line with `spf=` |
| A retried delivery of the same mail is held twice | A relay prepends its own `Received`, ARC and `Authentication-Results` lines on a retry, so a hash of the raw bytes differs | The delivery key hashes the authored headers, the DKIM `b=` values and the body (`deliveryKey`, `lib/inbound-mail/mime.ts`), never the raw bytes |
| `review-first.spec.ts` fails after a merge with a chain such as `inbound-mail/suggest-job.ts -> … -> upload-batches/settle.ts -> … -> documents/service.ts`, though no mail code changed | `lib/documents/service.ts` exports `uploadDocument`, so every module that imports anything from it is filing code to the walk, dynamic imports included. A read added beside it (the upload report reading the findings, which read `listDocumentsPage`) puts the whole file behind the receive side | Import the read from a module that does not file (`lib/documents/shelf-page.ts` holds `listDocumentsPage`); never exempt the chain in the spec |
| A GIN (or `text_pattern_ops`) index on a tenant table shows `idx_scan = 0` in `pg_stat_user_indexes` although the search filters on that column all day; `EXPLAIN` as the owner or `app_platform` uses it, `EXPLAIN` as `app_rw` inside `withTenant` shows a `Filter:` above a scan on the organization index | Row-level security: the planner never evaluates a non-leakproof operator before the policy's qual, so it cannot be an index condition below it. `@>`, `&&`, `@@`, `LIKE` and most functions are not leakproof; `=`, `<`, `IN` on plain types are. Marking an operator `LEAKPROOF` needs a superuser, which no migration has | An index for the runtime role is a B-tree on leakproof comparisons, leading with `organization_id`. Before adding a GIN to a tenant table, `EXPLAIN` the query as `app_rw` with the tenant set (`set_config('grid.organization_id', …, true)`), not as the owner. Migration 0185 dropped five such indexes from `document_search_facets` (measured in `src/lib/search/search-latency.integration.spec.ts`) |
| A grouped count in a CTE or subquery is as slow as if it had never grouped: `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows the outer `WHERE` (a `LIKE` on an expression of the grouped column) as a `Filter:` on the scan under the `GroupAggregate`, evaluated once per input row instead of once per group | Postgres pushes a qual on a GROUP BY column below the aggregation, and inlines a plain CTE. The search's completions folded every party of 18,000 documents (`translate()` per row, 290 of 316 ms) before counting them per spelling | Make the grouped step `WITH … AS MATERIALIZED (…)`: the fence keeps the expensive expression on the distinct values. `suggestStatement` in `apps/workspace/bff/search/suggest-repository.ts`; `SUGGEST_EXPLAIN=1` on the latency spec prints its plan |
| A query fails with `invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00` (`22021`, `where: unnamed portal parameter $n`), or `null character not permitted` from `chr(0)` | Postgres `text` cannot hold NUL, so a JS key joined with `\0` cannot be a parameter. A unit spec with a mocked database never sends it | Join with a character text can hold: the search's content keys use U+001F through `contentKey()` in `lib/search/document-repository.ts`, proved by `document-repository.integration.spec.ts`. `\0` is fine for a key that only ever reaches a hash (`draft-filing.ts`, `confirmation.ts`) |
| PostHog error tracking fills with `Error: NEXT_REDIRECT` for ordinary navigation | Next's `redirect()` and `notFound()` work by throwing; the browser's unhandled-rejection capture and the server's `onRequestError` reported them as errors | Closed: one shared predicate drops framework control flow in `capturePosthogException`, the browser `before_send` and the server `beforeCapture` (`bff/analytics/posthog.ts`, 18d77faa8). A new capture path calls the same predicate. [`error-tracking.md`](../observability/error-tracking.md) |
| React error #418 (hydration mismatch) on a page that lists times, such as Dateien | The server renders the pre-mount time in its own zone (UTC) and the browser in the person's (Vienna), so the text differs | Render the pre-mount absolute time in a fixed zone, `OFFICE_TIME_ZONE` from `@/lib/format`, as `ui/components/ui/time-ago.tsx` does (18d77faa8) |
| Dateien (or another page) of a closed project goes to the error boundary on open, while open projects work | A helper that promises never to throw a denial caught only `NotFoundError`; a closed project (ADR-0090) refuses a write check with its own `ForbiddenError`, which escaped (#1096, fixed in #1103) | A "may this person write?" helper treats the closed-project refusal as a plain no, as `decide.ts` does, and rethrows anything else. | Release-notes commits from before 2026-10-29 (`docs(changelog): publish release notes to the website`) changed no page | The bot then wrote the retired site's `src/data/changelog.json`, which no image served (the `web` image builds `apps/website`, `ci/image_inputs.py`). It now writes `apps/website/src/data/changelog.json`, rendered at `/changelog/` | Read the diff, not the subject: a commit subject can name a destination its diff does not touch. When a bot writes into a directory, check that a built image reads that path ([`release-notes.md`](release-notes.md)) |
| Filing an attachment of a held mail fails at the filing job with `inbound_mail_parts_suggestion_with_bytes` (`new row … violates check constraint`), but only for an attachment the agent's suggestion placed | `markAttachmentFiled` cleared the part's `storage_key` and `filename` and left its `suggestion`, and the CHECK says a suggestion names a file and goes with its bytes | Fixed: the filed update clears `suggestion` with the bytes (`bff/inbound-mail/filing-repository.ts`); `review-first.e2e.spec.ts` files a suggested attachment against Postgres. A unit spec with a mocked repository never sees a CHECK |
| A mail's text stays `filing` for good (no `lastFailure`, the mail's `updatedAt` frozen), its attachments filed, and a `<subject>.md` that no E-Mail-Verlauf points to sits in the folder the agent suggested; the filing job's retries fail with `inbound_mail_parts_suggestion_with_bytes` | The same CHECK as the row above, on the text: `commitAppend` set the message part `filed` and left its `suggestion`, so the whole commit rolled back after the Verlauf's file was uploaded. Only a text the agent placed has a suggestion; one filed into the root or an existing Verlauf never tripped it | Fixed: `commitAppend` clears `suggestion` with the `filed` update (`bff/mail-thread/write-repository.ts`); `append.integration.spec.ts` files a suggested text against Postgres. A stuck part goes back to waiting when sweep 5 reclaims its claim; its orphan `.md` holds the name, so delete that file before filing the text again |
| An inbound-mail integration spec signs a reply for real and still reads `not_addressed_to_project` (no follow queued, `expected [] to have a length of 1`) | The project address is `<local part>+<slug>.<token>@<domain>` (`address.ts`, `INBOUND_MAIL_LOCAL_PART`, `project` by default); a To of `<slug>.<token>@<domain>` names no project, so the signed recipients do not either | Write the fixture's To as `project+<slug>.<token>@<domain>`, as `hostile.PROJECT_ADDRESS` does |
| A `text-name`, `text-meta` or `text-section` class does nothing: the name renders at the inherited 14–16px, and the computed `font-size` is not the token's, while the class is in the source | `cn()` is tailwind-merge, which reads an unknown `text-*` as a colour. Beside `text-foreground` or `text-muted-foreground` it kept the colour and dropped the size, silently. The first Paper on a desk pass worked around it with `!text-[length:var(--text-section)]` | Closed: `TYPE_SCALE` in `bff/utils.ts` teaches `cn()` the type scale, and `bff/utils.spec.ts` fails when a `--text-*` size in `tokens.css` is missing from it. A new size goes in both. A raw `className` string (no `cn`) never had the problem |
| A table's columns collapse into its left half and the name cell truncates after a few letters, right after a row got a `relative` class and an `after:` pseudo-element (a hairline) | Chrome lays out a `::after` of a `<tr>` as an anonymous table cell, even absolutely positioned: it took the width the `table-fixed` columns were meant to share | Draw a row's line on its cells: `TABLE_ROW_HAIRLINE` (`ui/components/ui/row-hairline.ts`) paints a 1px background on each `td`. | Rows wrapped one by one in another element (a context menu's trigger box, `ActionMenu`'s `h-full min-w-0` div) all lose their bottom hairline | `last:` matches every row, since each is the last child of its own wrapper | Give the menu `asChild` (`FolderObjectMenu asChild`, `DocumentObjectMenu asChild`), so the row stays its list's own child and `last:` finds the last one. Round 4 retired the `WRAPPED_ROWS_LAST` workaround with the folder block it served |
| A paper token changed in `tokens.css` and the page did not move, or the header band and the page under it are two shades of paper | Three tokens paint „the page“: `--background` (`bg-background`, the section header), `--background-color-surface-base` (`bg-surface-base`, the `<body>`) and the brand's `--ds-surface`, which both used to read directly; `--paper` fed neither | The Paper on a desk block maps both to `--paper` (`tokens.css`), and „the browser-chrome colours are the app's paper“ in `brand-literals.spec.ts` holds `--background: var(--paper)`. Change the paper there, never `--ds-surface` (the website's) |
| On a phone the detail view's columns collapse: the name cell shows three letters („…tz“) and the ⋯ floats mid-row, as soon as the column head is hidden below a width | `table-fixed` takes its column widths from the table's FIRST row. With the `<thead>` `display: none` (the lone „Name“ head on a phone) the first row is a body row, and a cell without a width there (the actions cell) is sized from nothing | Every fixed column's cell carries its width in the body rows too (`w-10` on the actions cells of `file-list-view.tsx` and `folder-table-row.tsx`), not only its `<th>` |
| CI fails `app/manifest.spec.ts` („THEME_COLOR is still the --background token of each theme“: expected `#f6f5f3`, received `#fafaf9`) after the paper moved | The spec took the first `--background:` lines with a hex comment, the brand's, which the Paper on a desk block overrides further down with `var(--paper)` and no hex | Fixed: the spec reads the cascade's answer, the last `--background` (`var(--paper)`) and each theme's `--paper`. Retune `THEME_COLOR` with `--paper` |
| A ratchet meant for the phone-only classes (`sm:hidden`) also matches the desk-only ones, and the count is off by one | `\bsm:hidden\b` matches inside `max-sm:hidden`: `-` is a word boundary | Anchor a Tailwind variant with `(?<![\w-])`, as the spec's other class checks do |
| A scrolling column with `scroll-fade-bottom` shows a white (or the dialog's) band at its foot although the column's own background is the desk | The fade is a `mask-image` on the column, and a mask hides the element's own background with its content: what shows through is whatever lies under the column | Paint the plane on the parent the column sits in (`file-preview-pane-body.tsx` paints the desk under both preview columns), never only on the masked element |
| A link to `/app/projects/<id>/chat?session=<other>` followed while already on that chat route jumps straight back to the open chat | `useSessionUrl` reads `?session=` only on arrival; after that it writes the store's open chat into the URL, so a changed URL with an unchanged store is „corrected“ back | Select the chat in the store before the link is followed (`selectConversation`, as the history sheet and the rail's `RailChatsProvider` do); the URL then agrees with it |
| A page title sits 8px right of its crumb at desk width, though nothing is drawn before it | `PageHeader`'s `titleStart` slot rendered its wrapper while the back link inside was `hidden` at that width; an empty flex item still takes the line's `gap-2` | Give the slot the same visibility as what it holds (`titleStartClassName`, as `ProjectSectionFrame` does) |
| A rule written in JsonLogic decides with a fact nobody has entered: `{"<=": [{"var": "storeys_above_ground"}, 3]}` is `true` on an empty profile, and a Building with no storeys comes out low | `json-logic-js` (and `json-logic-engine`) read a missing `var` as `null` and compare on; `null <= 3` is true in JavaScript. Neither has a three-valued mode | Never run a ruleset's conditions through a JsonLogic library directly. Use `evaluateDecisionTable` in `apps/workspace/bff/base-knowledge/rulesets/` (ADR-0166): a missing fact is unknown, and the answer is `undetermined` with the facts it waits for. The library is the specs' oracle on complete facts only |
| A stored answer asked through the agent door (`metadata.agentConsult` on the question) shows no Herleitung after a reload, while the same question asked in the browser does; `conversation-message-list` with `include=steps` returns no `thinkingSteps` | The Herleitung hangs on the QUESTION's `provenance.thinkingSteps`, and only the browser that watched the turn writes them (`_persistTurnProvenance` in `ui/features/chat/stores/sessions-store.ts`); `chat-message-list.tsx` draws `ChatThinking` only when the question has steps. A door ask has no such browser, so its answer keeps citations and `retrievalLedger` but no steps | To review what such an answer's Herleitung says, feed the stored payload to `/dev/derivation-records` (the mapper and `ChatThinking`, steps empty). Persisting the steps server-side is the fix, not yet built |

## Sign-in and sessions

| Symptom | Cause | Do this |
|---|---|---|
| Sign-in on a deployed app bounces or loops: WorkOS returns to `/api/auth/callback`, which answers `Sign-in session could not be verified` (logs: `[AuthKit callback error] … missing_pkce_cookie`), and `user_signed_in` repeats in PostHog with no `$pageview` | AuthKit's proxy mints the PKCE verifier cookie only when the redirect answers an **initial document** navigation (`isInitialDocumentRequest`, `pkce.ts` — the same predicate through 4.6.0). A client-side (RSC) navigation and a client fetch get the same 307 to WorkOS, without the verifier. The client router then makes it a loop rather than a dead end: a redirected RSC fetch whose answer is not a Flight payload becomes an MPA navigation to the **redirect target** (`doMpaNavigation(responseUrl)` in Next's `fetch-server-response.js`), so the browser lands on WorkOS and starts a flow that was never given a verifier. `handleAuth` deletes the flow cookie before answering and returns a generic error page, so reloading the callback URL fails identically | `rerouteIncompleteSignIn` in `apps/workspace/proxy.ts` replaces a verifier-less authorize redirect with `/api/auth/signin?returnPathname=…`, a Route Handler where `cookies().set()` is legal whatever kind of request it answers; the callback's `onError` sends `missing_pkce_cookie`/`oauth_state_mismatch` to `/auth/error?error=SessionExpired`, which offers Try Again. Check the redirect before blaming the browser: `curl -sI https://<app>/app/projects -H 'accept: text/html' \| grep -i set-cookie` must name `wos-auth-verifier`; repeat with `-H 'RSC: 1' -H 'accept: text/x-component'`, where it is legitimately absent and the redirect must go to `/api/auth/signin` rather than WorkOS |
| A new browser, a private window or a cleared one loops between the app and WorkOS until it shows `ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS`; every callback succeeds, nothing is logged, and a browser that was already signed in keeps working | The sealed `wos-session` cookie grew past the browser's 4096 bytes and was dropped without a word: the access token carries every permission and feature flag WorkOS grants (18 and 33 for a GRID Test admin, about 4,180 bytes sealed). The proxy logs `[auth] session cookie present, but AuthKit is sending the request to sign in again` with `cookieBytes` when a cookie arrives and is not used | Closed by splitting the cookie (`patches/@workos-inc%2Fauthkit-nextjs@*.patch`, [authentication-flow.md](../technical-reference/authentication-flow.md#the-session-cookie-and-why-it-is-split)); `session-cookie-chunks.spec.ts` fails when an upgrade drops the patch. In a browser, DevTools → Application → Cookies shows `wos-session.0`, `wos-session.1` for a large session. Upgrading AuthKit means re-creating the patch, or deleting it once workos/authkit-nextjs#404 is fixed |
| A WorkOS redirect URI, resource indicator, logout URI or CORS origin that someone added earlier the same day is gone, and the ids of every entry in that list are new | The WorkOS write operations (`setRedirectUris`, `setAuthkitOauthResources`, `setLogoutUris`, `updateCorsConfig`) replace the whole list. A write built from a list read earlier silently drops whatever another person or agent added in between: on 2026-11-05 a CMS change rewrote staging's resource indicators from a read taken before the domain move added `app.dev.archos.tech` | Read the list right before each write, in the same breath, and keep every entry you did not mean to remove. When two sessions configure one environment, tell each other before writing. Fresh ids on every entry are the sign a list was rewritten, not edited |
| With `REQUIRE_AUTH=false`, `/app/memory` and every other page behind a session answer 500, and `/api/user/*` answers `403 {"error":"Forbidden"}`; only `/dev/*` and a few pages render. `.env.example` used to promise a „Default User“ | That mode has no session. `withPageSession` and `apiRoute` call `requireUserSession`, which throws `SessionRequiredError` when AuthKit has no user; there is no anonymous server path (the UX audit's FLAG-1) | To run the app signed in without a WorkOS account, use the [local stack](local-stack.md): `REQUIRE_AUTH=true`, a test double of the WorkOS API and a minted session cookie |

## On a phone

| Symptom | Cause | Do this |
|---|---|---|
| A region of a page swallows every swipe — the finger moves, the page does not, but taps still work | Something in the ancestor chain sets `touch-action` to a value that refuses the vertical pan, usually a third-party stylesheet claiming a gesture. React Flow's `.react-flow__pane` did exactly that to the whole reasoning graph, for pan and zoom the graph had already disabled in its props | The browser intersects `touch-action` over the touched element **and every ancestor**, so reading the computed style of the thing you touched tells you nothing — walk outward. The audit that reported these as `SCROLL TRAP` (`task workspace:touch-audit`) was deleted with the screenshot harness, so this one is back to walking the chain by hand. Scope the override to a class the component opts into, so a component that really does own the gesture keeps the default |
| `truncate` does nothing and a list scrolls sideways instead | The cell is in a `table-layout: auto` table, where a column is sized to its content's **minimum** — and a filename does not wrap, so the minimum is the whole name | `table-fixed` plus a declared width on every column but one. Hiding columns at `sm`/`md` does not help: it removes the columns that were not the problem |
| A first tap on a chip or card does nothing and the second one works, or tabbing onto a control opens its panel and focus falls back to the page | Something the control's first interaction sets (an `engaged` flag, a lazily mounted popover) changed the element TYPE that wraps it: `<button>` became `<Popover><PopoverAnchor><button>`. React remounts an element whose position in the tree changes, so the focused node or the node the finger went down on is detached, and a remount between `pointerdown` and `click` swallows the tap. Tests using `fireEvent.click` alone never see it | Never wrap the trigger on engagement. Render the lazy part as a SIBLING after it and anchor by ref: `useHoverPopover` + `HoverPeekPanel` (`components/ui/hover-peek-panel.tsx`, `PopoverAnchor virtualRef`). Pin it with a spec that holds the node before engaging and asserts `toBe` the same node, `document.activeElement` included — `hover-peek-panel.spec.tsx` is the worked example |
| Tapping a control does something, but not the thing under the finger | Two `touch-target` catchments overlap, and the later element in the DOM takes the tap | The utility is for a control with room around it. Stacked rows and inline neighbours must GROW (`pointer-coarse:py-*`) instead: stacked ~33px apart, two 44px catchments overlap by ~11px and the later row takes the tap |
| A `<button>` used as an inline chip in running prose renders its own text centred, but only once it wraps | The UA stylesheet gives `button` `text-align: center`. Every chip short enough to fit one line looked fine, so this surfaced only when a 44-character filename broke across two lines and the second half sat centred in a left-aligned paragraph | `text-start` on the chip. While you are there: `inline-flex` cannot break at all, so a long label is pushed to a line of its own and the sentence's own full stop wraps after it — an inline chip that holds text wants `display: inline` plus `[box-decoration-break:clone]`. `features/chat/components/FileReference.tsx` is the worked example |
| iOS zooms into the page when a field is focused and never zooms back out | A text field under 16px. The primitives carry `text-sm … pointer-coarse:text-base`; a hand-written `<input>` inherits none of it. The floor is on the POINTER axis: built out of a breakpoint (`text-base … md:text-sm`) it is not a floor at all, because a coarse-pointer tablet past `md` renders 14px and zooms anyway | `apps/workspace/ui/components/ui/mobile-affordances.spec.ts` fails on this now. Use the primitive, or carry the same floor |
| „Verbindung kurz unterbrochen — Antwort ging verloren" on an iPhone, for an answer that finished on the server | iOS closes a page's WebSocket as soon as the app goes to the background. The client used to end the turn at the first `onclose`, then drop every later frame as stale, and the server skipped persisting the answer because a socket was attached ("the client owns the write") | Closed in two layers: the server persists every finished answer, socket or not; and every event is in the conversation's stream on Dragonfly with its turn's `seq`, so the reopened socket `attach`es the turn from the last `seq` it folded and the turn stays open across the drop. A page that still finds no answer waits while the turn's heartbeat is fresh (`_awaitServerAnswer`) instead of saying "lost". [`streaming-chat-answer.md`](../design/streaming-chat-answer.md#a-dropped-socket-resumes). A resume answered `rejected{turn_not_found}` for a turn that is still running: check the agent has `REDIS_URL` and not `CONVERSATION_BUS=0`, without which nothing is in the stream |
| A public site renders every section off-centre on a phone, with an empty gutter on the right; `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` is larger than the phone (429 on a 390px screen) | One decorative element wider than the page — a blurred glow at `max-w-[120%]`, a header row that did not fit — widens the mobile LAYOUT viewport, and the whole page is laid out at that width. `overflow-x: clip` on `body` does not stop it: body's overflow is handed to the viewport, where mobile Chrome still lets the layout grow | Seen on the retired public site, closed there by clipping `main` and `footer` (`overflow-x: clip`, never `hidden`, which would break `position: sticky`); on the website `bun run capture` reports overflow per route. Still fix the element that overflows; to find it, list every element whose `getBoundingClientRect().right` exceeds `clientWidth` with an emulated phone (`isMobile: true`), since a desktop viewport at 390px hides the symptom |
| A control is invisible on a phone but present in the DOM and focusable | `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` with no touch escape — a touch device generates no hover | Add `pointer-coarse:opacity-100`, or invert to `md:opacity-0` so it is visible below the breakpoint. Also guarded by `apps/workspace/ui/components/ui/mobile-affordances.spec.ts` |
| On an iPhone the composer sits behind the keyboard, and Safari pans the whole page up to reveal the field, half the shell's chrome off screen | iOS Safari ignores `interactiveWidget: 'resizes-content'` (`app/layout.tsx`): the keyboard shrinks only the VISUAL viewport, and `100dvh` keeps the full screen. Chromium honours it, so no desktop or headless run shows this | `useVisualViewport` publishes the visual viewport's height as `--visual-viewport-height` while the keyboard covers part of it (not for pinch zoom), the shell sizes itself `h-[var(--visual-viewport-height,100dvh)]`, and the hook scrolls the document back to the top to cancel Safari's pan. Check a change here on a real iPhone; it cannot be emulated headless |

## Documentation and agent setup

| Symptom | Cause | Do this |
|---|---|---|
| `ruff format --check` fails with `File would be reformatted` on a `.md` file, or `ruff format` dedents an indented Python snippet in a doc | Since 0.16, ruff formats ```` ```python ```` blocks inside Markdown, at the same 120 columns as the code. It dedents an indented excerpt, and a `.md` edit can fail the format check | Run `ruff format` and commit the result. For a snippet whose indentation or layout is the point, put `<!-- fmt:off -->` on its own line before the fence and `<!-- fmt:on -->` after it. The marker must be bare: `<!-- fmt:off: reason -->` is ignored, so give the reason in a separate comment. `docs/plans/2026-06-27-grid-oib-cards-and-devcontainer.md` at `56ee3329` is the worked example. A changed builtin `SKILL.md` also needs `node apps/workspace/scripts/sync-platform-skills.mjs`, and a changed `skills/` file needs `task agents:audit` |
| apm rejects `agents` as a target | `agent-skills` is the manifest name for the shared `.agents/skills/` path; `agents` appears only in the CLI's `--target` help | Use `agent-skills` in `apm.yml` |
| Per-turn LLM cost grows with the size of a project | Something builds a prompt block by iterating documents, bindings or memories with no bound. It is paid on every turn, including chit-chat | Carry the shape, not the list: one line per category with a count, and a route to the members. `buildDocumentRolesSection` and the folded Basiswissen shelf in `render_inventory_block` are the worked examples. A constant corpus is the clearest case: it is identical on every turn, so retrieval reaches it and the prompt need only say how many |
| The agent answers a "which files do I have" question confidently and wrongly | A cap shortened the list on its way into the prompt, and the block presented the remainder as the whole shelf. At the time listing turns were routed `meta`, which bound no search tools, so there was no retrieval to fall back on (that partition is gone, ADR-0052; the cap rule stands) | A cap says so in the text the model reads, per group, not only in the operator's log. `render_inventory_block` is the worked example |
| The assistant says a tool `ist in dieser Sitzung nicht verfügbar` although the config declares it | Historically the intent classifier's meta partition: a turn labelled `meta` was bound only the interaction tools, so `surface_documents` was missing on exactly the listing questions that needed it. Removed in [ADR-0052](../adr/0052-one-answering-agent-no-intent-router.md); every turn binds the full set now | Today the sentence means the tool is genuinely absent from the agent's `tools:` list in the config (or undeclared under `functions:`). `packages/agent/tests/test_config_tool_wiring.py` is the gate; add the two YAML lines |
| A control or overlay that sits on top of RENDERED CONTENT (a PDF page, an image, a drawing) is unreadable in one theme | The token that themes it follows the APP's surface, but the thing it composites against is the content, which is paper-white in both themes. A transparent `outline` button put near-white text on white paper; a highlight tint lifted for charcoal came out a third as strong over the same paper | Give it its own opaque surface (`bg-card`), or carry a per-theme strength so the COMPOSITE matches rather than the mix. `--passage-strength` in `globals.css` is the worked example |
| Small controls in a row float mid-height with dead space above and below them, and a form beside them hangs off the row's right edge | A `flex flex-wrap items-center` row got one item that grows tall — a disclosure that opens, a form. `items-center` centres every short item against it, and a right-aligned item's contents start wherever the spacer left off. The answer footer did this the moment a reader voted „nicht hilfreich" | Hand the row TWO items instead of one tall one: the control stays a fixed-height item, and what it opens is a sibling with `w-full`, which can share a line with nothing and so takes the next one at the row's left edge. `AnswerFeedback`'s `compact` mode is the worked example. A state that only appears after an interaction needs a `/dev` fixture in the row it ships in, not only in the component's own gallery — there is no row to break there |
| An affordance is missing in production but present in every preview and every test you look at | Its condition ANDs in something a preview fixture leaves out. The answer's plain copy button was gated on `!hasSources && !canExport`, and `canExport` is true for every persisted turn — so it was visible in the gallery, where fixtures carry no conversation id, and gone in the product on every uncited answer | Read the condition as the production case, where the optional props are all present. When two affordances are alternatives, gate them on the ONE fact that distinguishes them and let the unrelated ones stand on their own; a download is not a clipboard copy |
| A Tailwind colour class renders with no fill and nothing warns | The class used a slash-opacity modifier (`bg-warning/15`) against a **static** Tailwind v4 `@utility`, which matches literally, so the class matches no rule and is dropped | `node scripts/check-static-utility-modifiers.mjs`, wired into `bun run lint` |
| The user had to say "keep going" | The agent stopped at a boundary to ask permission for reversible work that was already agreed | Predict the answer before asking. If it is "yes", proceed and report instead. See [working-style.md](working-style.md#finish-the-task) |
| A scoped `AGENTS.md` did not apply until halfway through the session | Claude loads a nested `CLAUDE.md` only after it **reads a file** in that directory, and never for a question answered without opening one | Open the scope's guide yourself rather than waiting for the harness: `git ls-files '*AGENTS.md'` lists every one. See [`AGENTS.md`](../../AGENTS.md#where-the-scoped-guides-are) |
| A tool you wrote, registered and documented is never called, and no test objects | the runtime binds only what a config declares under `functions:` AND lists in an agent's `tools:`. `view_knowledge_image` was built, `@register_function`-ed, eagerly imported, ADR'd and documented as default-on, and declared in no config for its whole life — so the model read captions OF plans and never a plan | `packages/agent/tests/test_config_tool_wiring.py` reads the shipped config as data and fails on an agent listing an undeclared tool. When you add a runtime function, add it there too — the build, `tsc` and every behavioural test are all silent about the two missing YAML lines |
| „Erneut prüfen" on a quarantined upload answers an error, and the log shows SQLSTATE `GQH01` (before migration 0225) | `markForRescreening` returned the row to `uploaded` without a release, and the trigger of migration 0122 let a quarantined row leave only WITH a release. It had only ever been mock-tested | Fixed in migration 0225: a quarantined row may also return to `uploaded` when the new row says `screening_outcome = 'quarantined'` and `content_hash`, `screened_hash` and `screening_released_hash` are unchanged, which is what the re-screen writes. Not "when nothing changes": no CHECK ties the status to the outcome, and a quarantined row still carrying an earlier `clean` would have passed the gate. `visibility.integration.spec.ts`, „a re-screen of a quarantined file" |
| A compare on a `timestamptz` parameter matches no row although the value was read from that very column a moment ago | postgres.js sends a string parameter cast straight to `timestamptz` cut to milliseconds, and the column holds microseconds | Read the token as text (`claimed_at::text`) and compare `$1::text::timestamptz` (`apps/workspace/purger/db.js`, the deletion claim token). `folder-bin.integration.spec.ts`, „a claim is a token" |
| A document shows „Fehlgeschlagen" right after it was read again, archived or replaced, and a Word or IFC file's new bytes are never read | Until November 2026 the stuck-row sweep (`recoverStuckProcessing`) and the extraction jobs wrote `failed` for work that began earlier without re-checking the row: the sweep worked a batch it had read up to 50 dispatches before, and a job checked `jobStillOwnsRow` only before its work | Fixed at the cause: `markDocumentIngestFailed` takes what the caller owned (`{ storageKey, backgroundJobId }`) and writes only while the row is still `processing` under it; a refused write is counted as gone. Callers that fail a read they are starting pass nothing and write as before |
| A folder sits in the Papierkorb as waiting to be deleted for ever, or `GET /api/deletions` shows a `pending` deletion that never moves; `SELECT id, entity_type, attempts FROM deletion_queue WHERE status = 'pending' AND attempts >= 10` (as the owner or `app_platform`: RLS hides the rows from `app_rw`) returns rows | Until November 2026 a request-side claim („Endgültig löschen") could leave a row `pending` at or past the purger's attempt cap, where the purger's claim (`attempts < 10`) never takes it: after a failed manual purge of a row the purger had given up on, or after a successful one that handed the trace erasure back to the purger. The folder's documents, or the traces of what was removed, were never erased | Fixed at the cause: a request's release applies the purger's rule (`failed` at the cap), the hand-over resets the attempts, and since migration 0227 the database refuses the state (CHECK `deletion_queue_pending_has_attempts`); the migration gave each row stranded before the fix its last attempt back once, so those end `purged` or `failed` within about an hour of the deploy. (Between the fix and 0227 the purger did that repair on every tick, `reofferExhausted`.) If the query still returns rows, the constraint is missing: check `SELECT conname FROM pg_constraint WHERE conname = 'deletion_queue_pending_has_attempts'`. `purger/deletion-lifecycle.spec.mjs` holds the purger's literals and moves to `DELETION_LIFECYCLE`. [`deletion-pipeline.md`](../architecture/deletion-pipeline.md) |
| A deep-research run's block says „Läuft" for hours, its „Abbrechen" does nothing (the BFF answers 409 „already ended"), and the chat list keeps a spinner on the thread | A run ends in two records: the `task_runs` row, closed by the outcome report, and the block's ledger (`metadata.run_ledger`), which took its ending only from the worker's own fold, sent once and never retried. The ghost reaper, the cancel route (for a job no worker had claimed) and the worker's retry exhaustion all report an outcome with no worker left to send the ledger op, so the row closed and the block stayed live. The reconciler claims only active rows, so nothing looked again; the browser's localStorage copy never took the server's newer ledger either | Closed in three layers: `recordRunOutcome` closes the row first, by compare-and-set from its live states, and then settles a live ledger with the ending the row holds (`settleRunLedger`, under the message's row lock so a worker's richer ending is never overwritten); the first ending to close the row stands, and an ended ledger refuses a later status (`RUN_LEDGER_LIFECYCLE`, `TASK_RUN_LIFECYCLE`); the reconciler heals every closed run once after it ended (`claimClosedRunsToHeal`, migration 0099), which also catches any future path that closes a row some other way; and the store takes a newer server ledger (`withServerFacts`), while a refused „Abbrechen" re-reads the run. A second writer of `task_runs.status` must go through `recordRunOutcome`, or the heal is the only thing that settles its block |
| A root-level `pytest` in a worktree passes on `agent_host` code you changed without exercising it, or fails on a module only the worktree has | `agent_host` is a uv workspace member installed editable from the MAIN checkout, and pytest's `pythonpath` named only this checkout's `src/`, so `import agent_host` resolved to the main checkout's copy | `pyproject.toml` now puts `apps/agent-host/src` on pytest's `pythonpath` too. If a new workspace member ships tests, add its `src/` there in the same change |
| In a `git worktree` whose `.venv` is a symlink to the main checkout's, the commit hook **the Python packages keep their seams (import-linter)** fails with `No matches for ignored import knowledge_layer.vector_store.** -> qdrant_client` (or `agent.oib_sync -> knowledge_layer.ingest.adapter`), on a change that touched no Python | `lint-imports` builds its graph from the installed packages, and `agent`, `agent_host` and `knowledge_layer` are installed editable from the MAIN checkout. When that checkout is at another commit, the graph is its tree, and an `ignore_imports` line that matches the worktree matches nothing there | Put the worktree's own packages first: `knowledge_layer` maps to `packages/knowledge-layer/src`, so symlink it under a scratch directory (`ln -s $PWD/packages/knowledge-layer/src <scratch>/knowledge_layer`) and commit with `PYTHONPATH=<scratch>:$PWD/packages/agent/src:$PWD/apps/agent-host/src`. Never `--no-verify` it away: the same hook in CI reads the right tree |
| A route spec that sends many POSTs through the real `apiRoute` as one session starts failing with `429 RATE_LIMITED` halfway through | Every session mutation spends the `api-mutation` default bucket (`DEFAULT_MUTATION_LIMIT`, `src/lib/api/handler.ts`), and a spec that drives a route many times as one person drains it | Mock `enforceLimit` from `@/lib/limits/enforce` in that spec, as `src/app/api/proposals/[id]/route.spec.ts` does; never raise the limit for a test |
| A deep-research job flips to FAILURE with "Job timed out (no heartbeat received from worker)" within minutes of submission, and the run looked healthy | The ghost reaper (`routes/job_reaper.py`) fails a RUNNING job with no `job_events` row and no `job_info` write for 300 s (both, since 2026-11-11: read by its events alone, a run a retry had just taken over was reaped before its first event); the runner heartbeats every 30 s but only once `run_with_cancellation` is entered, so agent build, tool resolution and checkpointer open run unprotected, and the cancellation monitor treats the reaper's FAILURE as a stop signal within a second. Terminal-status stickiness then keeps the verdict | Look for what held the pre-run phase (a cold vector store, a slow checkpointer, a sync tool on the loop) before touching the thresholds; a slow provider call does not cause this, the heartbeat loop is independent of it |
| `AGENTS.md` is growing again | Project knowledge is being written where only ways of working belong | Give it a home under `docs/contributing/` and leave a one-line pointer. See [documentation.md](documentation.md) |
| A short outbound HTTP call to one host costs ~400 ms although the server answers in ~60 ms | An `httpx.AsyncClient` built per call pays the TLS handshake every time (measured 2026-09-22 on the decision endpoint: ~430 ms per-call client vs ~60 ms on a warm connection). Share one keep-alive client per event loop, as `common/decisions.py` does, and keep the per-call client only for a test transport |
| `agent-browser install` fails with `Failed to fetch version info` and a tunnel error in a cloud session | It downloads Chrome for Testing from a host the cloud network policy blocks. The failure is the policy, not the CLI | Skip the install. Use the Playwright Chromium the image ships: `export AGENT_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`. The SessionStart hook sets it for the session. [Cloud sessions](agent-skills.md#cloud-sessions-what-is-installed-and-how) |
| `.claude/skills` is empty or `apm` never ran in a cloud session, although the hook exists | The hook runs from the repository's `.claude/settings.json`, which Claude loads only for a session started on that one repository. A session with several repositories, or a clone the agent made itself, never triggers it | Run `bash archos/scripts/cloud-setup.sh --with-skills` from the directory above the clone, or `uvx --from apm-cli==$(awk '/^  APM_VERSION:/ {print $2}' Taskfile.yml) apm install --frozen` in the repository. Put `--with-skills` on the environment's Setup script to make it permanent. [Cloud sessions](agent-skills.md#cloud-sessions-what-is-installed-and-how) |
| A Hatchet worker logs `failed to register workflow` with `INVALID_ARGUMENT: tenant-scoped concurrency (name …) is not supported at the workflow level unless the workflow is a single task`, then no run is ever taken | The engine accepts a tenant-scoped `ConcurrencyExpression` (`is_tenant_scoped=True`) on a workflow only when it has one task. The SDK builds the proto without complaint and a fake client never sees it; only `put_workflow` against a real engine refuses | Put the limits on the task that does the work (`ingest_workflow.index_options`), not in `Hatchet.workflow(concurrency=…)`. The `Hatchet engine tests` CI job is what catches it |
| CI's **Hatchet engine tests** fail `test_research_workflow_engine.py` with a run that has no `test.started` event (`ValueError: not enough values to unpack`) and the log shows `WARNING:agent_host.jobs.event_store:Failed to store event … (sqlite3.OperationalError) database is locked` about 30 s after the run started | The engine tests share one SQLite job store, whose write lock is the whole file. `run_ownership.try_take` holds it across an `await` (its lock is a no-op `UPDATE`), and `EventStore.store` is synchronous: a second run on the same worker that writes an event on the event loop blocks the loop for SQLite's 30 s busy timeout, so the take never commits and the write is dropped. Measured 2026-11-03: 30.1 s and no event on the loop, 0.0 s off it. Postgres, production's store, locks one `job_info` row and never shows it | A test stand-in writes its events off the loop and reads each back (`_record`, `orchestrator_engine_worker.py`). The same suite's end-to-end test read the engine's status the moment the run forgot its id, which is still inside the step: wait for it to settle (`_hatchet_status_settles`) |
| An ingest job stays pending for ever right after a fresh deploy or a new workflow name, and Hatchet has no run for it (`runs.get_status` 404) although `trigger` returned a run id | The engine answers a trigger of a workflow no worker has registered with a run id and then creates nothing. A registration persists in the engine, so only the first triggers before the first ingest worker comes up meet this | `ingest_workflow.require_registered` asks `workflows.list` once per process and refuses with `WorkflowNotRegistered`, which `ingest_dispatch` treats as Hatchet away and runs the job itself. A new workflow must do the same before its first trigger |
| A platform publish's index runs fail with `the base corpus did not accept the file: Knowledge backend returned 500`, the base corpus files stay `pending` with `chunkCount 0`, and error tracking shows `QueueUnavailable` / `no worker has registered dev_ingest-document yet` | The base corpus hands its jobs to Hatchet only (`ingest_dispatch.enqueue_only`, no in-process fallback), and `require_registered` refuses until a worker has registered the workflow. A stack whose ingest tier sits at zero replicas never gets one: every corpus job is refused, the Hatchet backlog KEDA scales on stays empty, and no worker ever starts (dev, AT publish, 2026-11-12) | Keep `ingestWorkerMinReplicas` at 1 or more on every stack; `deploy/pulumi/src/app/ingest-worker.spec.ts` fails a stack file that lets it reach zero. Once a worker is up, `platform-knowledge-sync` queues the pending files with their stored marks; reingest again only meets the same refusal until then. The run's failure now carries the backend's own reason (`backendFailure` in `bff/knowledge/service.ts`) |
| A process that starts two `hatchet.worker(...)` (one on a thread, to give some tasks slots of their own) does not exit on SIGTERM, and one of the two keeps taking runs after it | Not supported by the Python SDK (1.44.0). Each `Worker` installs the process's signal handlers when it is built (`worker/worker.py`, `_setup_signal_handlers`), so only the one built last drains; `start` owns its thread's event loop and blocks, so the other worker's tasks also run on another loop than the lifespan's clients. Tried on engine v0.114.2, 2026-11-11 | One worker per process. Its only second slot pool is `durable_slots`, drawn on by durable tasks (`folder_cards/workflow.py`, whose header has the SDK lines); `slot_cost` charges the standard pool, and a worker label routes a run without reserving anything |
| A test under `apps/agent-host/tests` hangs about two minutes on any outbound connection, with a socket in `SYN_SENT` to `10.42.0.7` | The autouse `stub_private_dns` fixture answers every `getaddrinfo` with `10.42.0.7` for the ingest route's SSRF guard. It used to fake IP literals too, which sent real-engine REST calls for `127.0.0.1` there | IP literals now pass through to the real resolver. A test that needs a real host name resolved must override the fixture |
| After merging a branch written before the knowledge layer was laid out by job (2026-11), a test fails with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'knowledge_layer.search.qdrant'` (or another old path), although the merge reported no conflict there | The merge follows the renames and carries the branch's hunks into the moved files, but a hunk's import still names the old place: `from .qdrant.schema import …` inside `search/knowledge_search.py` resolves against the new folder | Point the import at the new package, by its surface where the name is public (`from knowledge_layer.vector_store import INDEXED_FIELDS`). `packages/knowledge-layer/tests/test_relative_imports_resolve.py` reads every relative import statically, function bodies included; for an absolute one, `scripts/check_package_surfaces.py` names the import to use |
| `Deploy (staging)` fails at **Validate plan (CRD schemas + policy pack)** with a bare `error: preview failed` and the reason nowhere in the JSON plan; CI on the PR was green | A mandatory rule of the policy pack (`deploy/pulumi/policy/index.ts`) refused a workload. The pack runs only in the deploy, which holds the stack's credentials, and it checks every object a `yaml/v2` ConfigFile or ConfigGroup carries, including a release manifest fetched by URL (a Helm release is one resource to it). The RabbitMQ operator's upstream Deployment, with no soak, was the first case | `deploy/pulumi/index-policy.spec.ts` applies the same mandatory workload rules to the dev stack's plan on every PR; it cannot see a manifest fetched by URL, so such a vendor Deployment goes in the pack's `UPSTREAM_SOAK_EXEMPT` with its reason. Reference the release by URL rather than copying it into the repo. The text-mode reason is printed before the JSON dump, early in the step's log |
| `Deploy (staging)` fails at **Validate plan** after the policy pack passed, with `FAIL admissionregistration.k8s.io/v1/ValidatingWebhookConfiguration` and `unexpected top-level field "webhooks" — did it belong under spec?` | `deploy/pulumi/scripts/validate-crs.mjs` checks every resource whose API group has a dot and is not in its `NATIVE_GROUPS`; a built-in group missing from that list is checked as a custom resource, and webhook configurations carry `webhooks` with no `spec` | Add the built-in group to `NATIVE_GROUPS`. Never match by the `.k8s.io` suffix: `gateway.networking.k8s.io` is a CRD group the script must still check. `validate-crs.spec.ts` holds the webhook case |
| `pulumi up` fails on the Helm release `hatchet` with `spec.template.spec.containers[0].ports[0].name: Invalid value: "hatchet-controllers": must be no more than 15 characters` | The `hatchet-ha` chart names each backend component's container port after its `nameOverride`, and Kubernetes caps a port name at 15 characters (IANA_SVC_NAME). Nothing before the apiserver checks it: preview, the policy pack and `validate-crs.mjs` all pass | Keep `HATCHET_COMPONENTS` (`deploy/pulumi/src/platform/hatchet.ts`) at 15 characters or fewer; the chart's own defaults are `controllers` and `scheduler`. `index-hatchet.spec.ts` checks the length |
| The Hatchet release sits in `updating` until its 1200s timeout; every `hatchet-api`, `hatchet-grpc`, `controllers` and `scheduler` pod crash-loops with `cannot declare queue: "task_processing_queue_v2", Exception (406) PRECONDITION_FAILED - invalid arg 'x-consumer-timeout' ... of queue type rabbit_classic_queue` | Hatchet declares classic queues with a hardcoded `x-consumer-timeout`; RabbitMQ 4.3 accepts it only on quorum queues. The operator's default image was 4.3.4. Not streams: `x-consumer-timeout` is no stream argument, and a missing `rabbitmq_stream` plugin is beside the point | Run the RabbitMQ Hatchet's compose file names (`RABBITMQ_IMAGE`, `platform/rabbitmq.ts`). Helm's wait cannot tell a crash loop from a slow start, so read the pod logs instead of waiting out the timeout |
| `pulumi up` fails on `inbound-mail-postmaster` and `inbound-mail-abuse` with `400 Bad Request`, code `2054`, "Destination address is not verified" | The project-mail forwards point at the operator's destination address, which the same apply just created; Cloudflare's API refuses a forward to it until someone clicks its verification mail, although the docs say such rules are created and kept disabled | Click the link Cloudflare mailed to `inboundMailOperatorAddress`, then re-run the failed `Pulumi up` job. Expected once per new destination address (runbook §7) |
| `pulumi up` waits on `Deployment clamav` until it times out; the pod's `freshclam` container is `OOMKilled` (exit 137) about 20 s after each start and sits in `CrashLoopBackOff` | freshclam's signature check (`TestDatabases`, on by default) loads the whole database as clamd does, so it needs clamd's memory, not a downloader's: 512Mi was too little on ClamAV 1.5.4 | `CLAMAV.freshclamResources` in `deploy/pulumi/src/clamav-constants.ts` (2Gi limit since 2026-11-05). On a new ClamAV version, check the peak again before lowering it |
| A pool on `lease_slots.hold` (the vision pool) admits more calls than its limit, or you are about to size a lease from a client timeout: `timeout=180` on the OpenAI SDK looks like "the call ends within 180 s" | It bounds no such thing. The SDK hands the number to httpx, where it is a timeout per phase (connect, each read, write, pool) of ONE attempt; `max_retries=1` is a second attempt after a backoff that honours `Retry-After`; and a wait inside our own transport (`openrouter._PinningTransport` queueing for a provider slot) is under no httpx timeout at all. A vision call under a 210 s lease could run for six minutes and more, and its slot aged out mid-call | `hold` renews its lease while the body runs (2026-11-11), so a lease is only how long a dead holder keeps a slot. Size nothing else from a client timeout either: measure the body, or renew |
| A document shows „Fehlgeschlagen" with `Ingestion could not be started` while it is being read, or stays so although its text is searchable; `metadata.ingestJobId` names a job the backend finished | Two dispatches of one row overlapped (a retry or a re-upload while the first request to `/v1/ingest` hung). The first gave up after its two 10 s timeouts and wrote `failed` over the second one's `pending`; a failed row is asked about by no push and no sweep. Until 2026-11-11 the dispatch's writes compared nothing | Fixed at the cause: `dispatchIngest` passes what it read of the row (`DispatchStart`) and both of its writes land only on a row with the same ingest job and storage key (`unchangedSinceDispatch`, `repository/mutations.ts`; `dispatch-failure-race.integration.spec.ts`). A row from before is healed by „Erneut lesen". A new writer that reports after waiting on the backend or a queue passes what it read or owned, never nothing |
| A document shows `failed` with `interrupted: …` although its text is searchable and its job ended `completed` | `interrupted` is a verdict about a job whose owner went silent, and the job can still finish. A read heals the row only for 30 minutes after the failure was written (`lost-job.ts`); a job that finished later (it waited in the queue that long because its hold was never written, or nobody opened the list) stayed failed, because the settle push looked for in-flight rows only | Closed 2026-11-11: the settle push also finds a row failed as `interrupted:` and re-reads its job with no window (`listSettleableDocumentsForIngestJobs`). A row from before, or one whose push was lost, is healed by „Erneut lesen", which finds the job done |

## Adding an entry

Add one the moment a failure costs you more than a few minutes, while you still
remember the symptom string. Three columns, and the symptom column is written as
what you would have searched for, not as a tidy summary after the fact.

Then ask the ratchet question: can this be closed rather than documented? A
CHECK constraint, a lint rule, or a failing test beats an entry here, and an
entry here beats nothing.

What happens to a row once its cause is fixed depends on whose defect it was:

- **This repo's tooling** (the build, CI, the dev environment, a script): fix it
  in code and delete the row in the same change. A row that only records a
  workaround for our own tooling is a bug report nobody closed.
- **Product behaviour**: keep the row and say what closed it. The symptom can
  come back as a regression, and this page is where somebody meeting it again
  searches first.
